Claim D.12

(Close-form formula for
r
)
.

\displaystyle r=R[l]^{\top}R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M^{\new}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}),

where
M^{\new}=A^{\top}(AW^{\appr}A^{\top})^{-1}A
.

Proof.

From Claim
D.8
, we have
r_{1}=Q[l]\sqrt{V}f(g)
.

From Claim
D.9
, we have
r_{2}=(Q[l]+R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M)\cdot(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))
.

From Claim
D.10
, we have
r_{3}=R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M\sqrt{V}f(g)
.

From Claim
D.11
, we have

\displaystyle r_{4}=-R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M_{S^{\new}}\cdot((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}\cdot(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}).

The proof sketch is as follows: we first compute
r_{1}+r_{3}
, then compute
(r_{1}+r_{3})+r_{2}
, and finally we compute
(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})+r_{4}
. First we compute
r_{1}+r_{3}
as follows:

\displaystyle r_{1}+r_{3}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ Q[l]\sqrt{V}f(g)+R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M\sqrt{V}f(g)=R[l]\sqrt{V}M\sqrt{V}f(g)+R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M\sqrt{V}f(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l](\sqrt{V}+(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma))M\sqrt{V}f(g)=R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{V}f(g),

(44)

where the first step follows from Claim
D.8
and
D.10
, the second step follows from
Q=R\sqrt{V}M
(Part
2
of Assumption
D.1
), the third step follows from merging terms, and the fourth step follows from
\Gamma+\partial\Gamma=(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})+(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})=\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}
, (
\Gamma
from Part
8
in Assumption
D.1
,
\partial\Gamma
from Part
2
in Definition
D.2
).

Secondly, we can compute
(r_{1}+r_{3})+r_{2}
,

\displaystyle(r_{1}+r_{3})+r_{2}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{V}f(g)+(Q[l]+R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M)(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{V}f(g)+(R[l]\sqrt{V}M+R[l](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M)(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{V}f(g)+R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}),

(45)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
D.1
) and Claim
D.9
, the second step follows from
Q=R\sqrt{V}M
(Part
2
of Assumption
D.1
), the third step follows from
\Gamma+\partial\Gamma=(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})+(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})=\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}
(
\Gamma
from Part
8
in Assumption
D.1
,
\partial\Gamma
from Part
2
in Definition
D.2
), and the fourth step follows from merging terms.

Finally, we can compute
r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4}
.

\displaystyle(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})+r_{4}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M_{S^{\new}}\left((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}\right)^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}\left(M-M_{S^{\new}}\left((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}\right)^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}\right)\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M^{\new}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}),

(46)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
D.1
) and Claim
D.11
, the second step follows from merging terms, and the third step follows from Lemma
C.8
(by setting the parameters in the lemma statement as
\Delta\leftarrow\Delta^{\new}
,
S\leftarrow S^{\new}
,
\widetilde{v}\leftarrow w^{\appr}
) and the definition that
M^{\new}=A^{\top}(AW^{\appr}A^{\top})^{-1}A
.

Therefore, from the assignment of
r
on Line
18
of Algorithm
12
, we have

r=R[l]^{\top}(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4})=R[l]^{\top}R[l]\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M^{\new}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}).

∎

D.2
Correctness of
UpdateV
and
UpdateG

Lemma D.13

(Correctness of
UpdateV
)
.

After executing the procedure
UpdateV
, the following properties are satisfied:

1.

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,
\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

2.

If all invariants of Assumption
D.1
are satisfied before entering the procedure
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
), then all invariants are still satisfied after
UpdateV
.

Proof.

Part 1.

The procedure
UpdateV
could exit in three places: Line
22
,
19
and
12
. We discuss them case by case.

(a.
Line
22
)
.
w^{\appr}
is assigned to be
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
. The algorithm avoids the if branches on Line
6
and the if branch on Line
14
. Therefore, both of the conditions of the if branches are false, so we have
\|\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v\|_{0}<n^{a}
and
\|\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}<n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

(b.
Line
19
)
.
w^{\appr}
is assigned to be
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
. The algorithm avoids the if branch on Line
6
, so we have
\|\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v\|_{0}<n^{a}
. Then the algorithm enters procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(see Line
16
) to update
\widetilde{v}\leftarrow\widetilde{v}^{\new}
(see Line
16
of Algorithm
14
), so
\|\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}=0<n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

(c.
Line
12
)
.
w^{\appr}
is assigned to be
v^{\new}
. The algorithm enters the procedure
MatrixUpdate
(see Line
9
) to update
v\leftarrow\widetilde{v}\leftarrow w^{\appr}
(see Line
13
of Algorithm
13
). Thus
\|v^{\new}-v\|_{0}=0<n^{a}
and
\|v^{\new}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}=0<n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

Part 2.
We first prove that
|S\cup\partial S|\leq 2n^{a}
is satisfied if we enter
PartialMatrixUpdate
. Note that the input
w^{\appr}
of
PartialMatrixUpdate
is
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
(Line
16
), so
\partial S=\supp(w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v})=\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v})
(Part
4
of Definition
D.2
). We have

\displaystyle|S\cup\partial S|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ |S|+|\partial S\backslash S|\leq n^{a}+|\partial S\backslash S|=n^{a}+|\{i\in[n]:v_{i}=\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}\neq\widetilde{v}_{i}\}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ n^{a}+|\{i\in[n]:\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}\neq v_{i}\}|\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ 2n^{a},

where the second step follows from
|S|\leq n^{a}
which is a direct implication of Part 1 of this lemma (see proof of Corollary
D.15
), the third step follows from
S=\supp(v-\widetilde{v})
(Part
5
of Assumption
D.1
) and
\partial S=\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v})
, and the fifth step follows from
|\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v)|<n^{a}
since the if-clause of Line
6
of
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
) has to be false to enter
PartialMatrixUpdate
.

In procedure
UpdateV
, the data structure members are modified only by procedure
MatrixUpdate
(on Line
9
) and procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(on Line
16
). Since all invariants are satisfied before entering
MatrixUpdate
or
PartialMatrixUpdate
, and
|S\cup\partial S|\leq 2n^{a}
is also satisfied before entering
PartialMatrixUpdate
, from Lemma
D.17
and Lemma
D.21
we know that all the invariants are still satisfied after
MatrixUpdate
and
PartialMatrixUpdate
.
∎

Lemma D.14

(Correctness of
UpdateG
)
.

After executing the procedure
UpdateG
, the following properties are satisfied:

1.

\|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,
\|h^{\appr}-\widetilde{g}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

2.

If all invariants of Assumption
D.1
are satisfied before entering the procedure
UpdateG
(Algorithm
10
), then all invariants are still satisfied after
UpdateG
.

Proof.

Part 1.
The proof is analogous to that of Part 1 of Lemma
D.14
.

Part 2.
In procedure
UpdateG
, the data structure members are modified only by procedure
VectorUpdate
(see Line
9
) and procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(see Line
15
). So this directly follows from the fact that all the invariants are satisfied after
VectorUpdate
(Lemma
D.25
) and
PartialVectorUpdate
(Lemma
D.29
).
∎

By the same reasoning, we immediately have the following corollary:

Corollary D.15

(Sparsity of
\widetilde{v}-v
and
\widetilde{g}-g
)
.

Throughout the algorithm the following is always satisfied:

\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|\widetilde{g}-g\|_{0}\leq n^{a}.

Corollary D.16

(Sparsity guarantees when entering the procedures)
.

.

Let
k
and
\widetilde{k}
be the output returned by
UpdateV
(Line
4
in Algorithm
8
).
Let
p
,
\widetilde{p}
be the output returned by
UpdateG
(Line
5
in Algorithm
8
).

1.

When entering the procedure
MatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
13
), we have

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}=k.

2.

When entering the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), we have

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}=\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}.

3.

When entering the procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
), we have

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}},\penalty\ \|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}=p.

4.

When entering the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
), we have

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}},\penalty\ \|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|h^{\appr}-\widetilde{g}\|_{0}=\widetilde{p}\leq 2n^{a}.

5.

When entering the procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), we have

\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}},\penalty\ \|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|h^{\appr}-\widetilde{g}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}.

Proof.

All line number mentioned in the proof is in
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
).

Part 1.

MatrixUpdate
is entered in Line
9
, and its input
w^{\appr}
is
v^{\new}
, which is defined on Line
7
. So
\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}=\|v^{\new}-v\|_{0}=k
directly follows from the definition of
k
.

Part 2.

PartialMatrixUpdate
is entered in Line
16
, and its input
w^{\appr}
is
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
, which is defined on Line
4
.
\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
is because the algorithm bypass the if-branch in Line
6
. And
\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}=\|\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v\|_{0}=\widetilde{k}
directly follows from the definition of
\widetilde{k}
. And
\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}+\|v-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq 2n^{a}
follows from triangle inequality and Corollary
D.15
.

Part 3,4.

The guarantee that
\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}\leq n^{a},\penalty\ \|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
is from Part 1 of Lemma
D.13
.
The remaining proof is the same as Part 1 and Part 2.

Part 5.
This directly follows from Part 1 of Lemma
D.13
and Part 1 of Lemma
D.14
.
∎

Claim D.19
.

In the procedure
MatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
13
), before we refresh variables in the memory of data structure, we have the following:

1.

M^{\mathrm{tmp}}=A^{\top}(AW^{\appr}A^{\top})^{-1}A
,

2.

Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}=R\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
,

3.

\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(g)
,

4.

\beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=M^{\new}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(g)
,

5.

\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}=\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(g)
.

Proof.

Part 1.

On Line
5
of Algorithm
13
we assigned
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
as

M^{\mathrm{tmp}}=M-M_{S^{\new}}\cdot((\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}\cdot(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}.

Since
M=A^{\top}(AVA^{\top})^{-1}A
(Part
1
of Assumption
D.1
),
\Delta^{\new}=W^{\appr}-V
(Part
7
of Assumption
D.1
), and
S=\supp(w^{\appr}-v)
(Part
5
of Assumption
D.1
), from Lemma
C.8
we have

M^{\mathrm{tmp}}=A^{\top}(AW^{\appr}A^{\top})^{-1}A.

Part 2.

We have

\displaystyle Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ Q+R(\Gamma^{\new}M^{\mathrm{tmp}})+R\sqrt{V}(M^{\mathrm{tmp}}-M)=R\sqrt{V}M+R(\Gamma^{\new}M^{\mathrm{tmp}})+R\sqrt{V}(M^{\mathrm{tmp}}-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R(\Gamma^{\new}+\sqrt{V})M^{\mathrm{tmp}}=R(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma+\sqrt{V})M^{\mathrm{tmp}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R((\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})+(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})+\sqrt{V})M^{\mathrm{tmp}}=R\sqrt{W^{\appr}}M^{\mathrm{tmp}},

where the first step follows from the assigned value for
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
6
of Algorithm
13
, the second step follows from
Q=R\sqrt{V}M
(Part
2
of Assumption
D.1
), the third step is by merging terms, the fourth step follows from
\Gamma^{\new}=\Gamma+\partial\Gamma
(Part
6
of Definition
D.2
), the fifth step follows from
\Gamma=\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V}
(Part
8
of Assumption
D.1
) and
\partial\Gamma=\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}
(Part
2
of Definition
D.2
), and the last step is by merging terms.

Part 3, 4 and 5.

These directly follow from the assignment of
\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
7
of Algorithm
13
, the assignment of
\beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
8
, and the assignment of
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
9
.
∎

Claim D.20

(Part 3 of Lemma
D.17
)
.

All invariants of Assumption
D.1
are satisfied after the procedure
MatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
13
).

Proof.

First note that
g
,
\widetilde{g}
, and
T
all remain the same after
MatrixUpdate
. Note that
v
and
\widetilde{v}
are both assigned the value
w^{\appr}
(Line
13
of Algorithm
13
). Then using Claim
D.19
, and since we assigned
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
M
,
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
Q
,
\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
\beta_{2}
, and
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to

, we have

We prove the other invariants directly from the assignment on Line

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=0=\Gamma M\cdot\xi,

\displaystyle E=0=B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\emptyset})^{\top}]=B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}],

\displaystyle F=0=R\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\emptyset}]=R\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S}].

Claim D.23
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
) before we refresh variables in the memory of the data structure, we have the following:

1.

B^{\mathrm{tmp}}=\mathcal{L}_{*}[((\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}]
,

2.

\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}=\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)
,

3.

\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S^{\new}}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
,

\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=\Gamma^{\new}M\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
,

,

.

Proof.

Part 1.
On Line

of Algorithm

, we assigned

as

B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}B,

where
(U^{\prime},C,U)=\textsc{Decompose}(\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}])
,
then using Lemma
C.6
we have that
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}=\mathcal{L}_{*}[((\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}]
.

Part 2 and 3.

Part 2 directly follows from the assignment of
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
10
of Algorithm
14
.

And Part 3 directly follows from the assignment of
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line

of Algorithm

.

Part 4.
We have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \gamma_{2}+(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})f(\widetilde{g})+\partial\Gamma M(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma M\cdot(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))+(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})f(\widetilde{g})+\partial\Gamma M(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma M\cdot(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))+\partial\Gamma M(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M\cdot(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma^{\new}M\cdot(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma^{\new}M\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}},

where the first step follows from the assignment of
\gamma_{2}^{\new}
(Line

of Algorithm

), the second step follows from

(Part
14
and
9
of Assumption
D.1
), the third and the fourth step both follow from merging terms, the fifth step follows from
\Gamma^{\new}=\Gamma+\partial\Gamma
(Part
2
of Definition
D.2
), and the sixth step follows from the Part 2 of this claim.

Part 5.

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ F+R\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S}]+R\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]+R\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ R\Gamma^{\new}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}],

where the first step follows from the assignment of

(Line

of Algorithm

), the second step follows from

(Part

of Assumption
D.1
), the third step follows from
S^{\prime}=(S\cup\partial S)\backslash S^{\new}
(Part

of Definition
D.2
) and thus

by Part
2
of Remark
C.3
, and the fourth step follows from
\Gamma^{\new}=\Gamma+\partial\Gamma
(Part
6
of Definition
D.2
).

Part 6

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ E+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}E

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (B-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}B)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]

where the first step follows from the assignment of
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}
(Line
9
of Algorithm
14
), the second step follows from
E=B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]
(Part
11
of Assumption
D.1
), the fourth step follows from the definition of
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, and the fifth step follows from
S^{\prime}=(S\cup\partial S)\backslash S^{\new}
(Part
9
of Definition
D.2
) and thus
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}]+\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]=\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S\cup\partial S})^{\top}]=\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]+\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]
by Part

of Remark
C.3
, and the fourth step follows from
\Gamma^{\new}=\Gamma+\partial\Gamma
(Part

of Definition
D.2
).
∎

16
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \gamma_{1}+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}](\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\Big((\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))+\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}},

where the second step follows from the invariant of
\gamma_{1}
(Part
13
in Assumption
D.1
), the third and the fourth steps follow from merging terms, and the last step follows from Part 1 of this lemma.

Part 3.
From the assignment of
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
6
of Algorithm
16
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \gamma_{2}+\Gamma M\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)=\Gamma M(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))+\Gamma M\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma M(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g))=\Gamma M\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}},

where the second step follows from the invariant of
\gamma_{2}
(Part
14
in Assumption
D.1
), the third step follows from merging terms, and the last step follows from Part 1 of this lemma.
∎

Claim D.32

(Part 3 of Lemma
D.29
)
.

All invariants of Assumption
D.1
are satisfied after the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
).

Proof.

First note that
v
,
\widetilde{v}
,
g
,
M
,
Q
,
B
,
\Delta
,
\Gamma
,
S
,
\beta_{1}
, and
\beta_{2}
all remain the same after the procedure
VectorUpdate
. Also note that
\widetilde{g}
is assigned the value
h^{\appr}
(Line
10
of Algorithm
16
).

Then using Claim
D.27
, and since we assigned
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
\xi
,
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
\gamma_{1}
, and
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
to
\gamma_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\xi,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma M\cdot\xi,

\displaystyle\xi=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g)=\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g).

Finally, from the assignment of
T
on Line
9
, we have
T=\supp(h^{\appr}-g)=\supp(\widetilde{g}-g)
.
∎

D.7
Correctness of
Initialize

Lemma D.33

(Correctness of
Initialize
)
.

When initialized, all the invariants of the data structure members stated in Assumption
D.1
are satisfied.

Proof.

Since in the beginning
v
and
\widetilde{v}
are both assigned the value
w_{0}
, and
g
and
\widetilde{g}
are both assigned the value
h_{0}
, it is obvious that
S=\emptyset
,
T=\emptyset
,
\Delta=0
,
\Gamma=0
,
\xi=0
,
\gamma_{1}=0
,
\gamma_{2}=0
all satisfy their invariant requirement of Assumption
D.1
. Also,
B=I=\mathcal{L}_{*}[0]
also satisfies the invariant requirement. Finally note that the initial assignment of
M
,
Q
,
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}
directly satisfy their invariant requirement of Assumption
D.1
.
∎

Lemma E.1

(Sparsity guarantees)
.

The members of data structure presented in Table
12
and Table
13
all follow the invariants of Assumption
D.1
, and the local variables presented in Table
13
all follow the definition of Definition
D.2
.
We have the following sparsity guarantees.

1.

When entering the procedures
MatrixUpdate
,
PartialMatrixUpdate
,
VectorUpdate
,
PartialVectorUpdate
, and
Query
, we have the sparsity bounds on
\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0}
,
\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0}
,
\|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}
,
\|h^{\appr}-\widetilde{g}\|_{0}
,
\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0}
, and
\|\widetilde{g}-g\|_{0}
as presented in Table
12
.

2.

When entering the procedures
MatrixUpdate
,
PartialMatrixUpdate
,
VectorUpdate
,
PartialVectorUpdate
, and
Query
, we have the sparsity bounds on
\Delta+\partial\Delta
,
\Gamma+\partial\Gamma
,
S\cup\partial S
,
\partial\Delta
,
\partial\Gamma
,
\partial S
,
\xi+\partial\xi
,
\partial\xi
,
\Delta
,
\Gamma
,
S
,
\xi
as presented in Table
13
.

Proof.

Part 1.

The first four columns follow from Corollary
D.16
, and the last two columns follow from Corollary
D.15
.

Part 2.
For Col. 1, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta+\partial\Delta\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|W^{\appr}-V\|_{0}=\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0},

\displaystyle\|\Gamma+\partial\Gamma\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0}=\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0},

the bounds of this column then follow from Col. 1 of Table
12
.

For Col. 2, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial\Delta\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|W^{\appr}-\widetilde{V}\|_{0}=\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0},

\displaystyle\|\partial\Gamma\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\|_{0}=\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0},

\displaystyle|\partial S|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|W^{\appr}-\widetilde{V}\|_{0}=\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0},

the bounds of this column then follow from Col. 2 of Table
12
.

For Col. 3 we have

\displaystyle\|\xi+\partial\xi\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g)\|_{0}\leq\max\{\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0},\|f(h^{\appr})-f(g)\|_{0}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max\{\|w^{\appr}-v\|_{0},\|h^{\appr}-g\|_{0}\},

the bounds of this column then follow from Col. 1 and Col. 3 of Table
12
and the fact
p\geq n^{a}
(since we have the equivalent fact for
p
as of Fact
F.10
for
k
).

For Col. 4 we have

\displaystyle\|\partial\xi\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})\|_{0}\leq\max\{\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\|_{0},\|f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\|_{0}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \max\{\|w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v}\|_{0},\|h^{\appr}-\widetilde{g}\|_{0}\},

the bounds of this column then follow from Col. 2 and Col. 4 of Table
12
and the fact
\widetilde{p}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
(since we have the equivalent fact for
\widetilde{p}
as of Fact
F.11
for
\widetilde{k}
).

For Col. 5 we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widetilde{V}-V\|_{0}=\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0},

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0}=\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0},

\displaystyle|S|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\supp(\widetilde{v}-v)|=\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0},

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)\|_{0}\leq\max\{\|\widetilde{v}-v\|_{0},\|\widetilde{g}-g\|_{0}\},

the bounds of this column then follow from Col. 5 and Col. 6 of Table
12
.

For the first part (
MatrixUpdate
) of Col. 6, we have

\displaystyle|S\cup\partial S|\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |S|+|\partial S|\leq|\supp(v-\widetilde{v})|+|\supp(w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\supp(v-\widetilde{v})|+|\supp(w^{\appr}-v)|+|\supp(v-\widetilde{v})|\leq n^{a}+k+n^{a}\leq 3k,

where the first and the third steps both follow from triangle inequality, the second step follows from
S=\supp(v-\widetilde{v})
(part
5
Assumption
D.1
) and
\partial S=\supp(w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v})
(Part
4
of Definition
D.2
), the fourth step follows from Col. 1 and Col. 5 of Table
12
, the last step follows from the fact that
k\geq n^{a}
(Fact
F.10
).

For the second part (
PartialMatrixUpdate
) of Col. 6, we have

\displaystyle|S\cup\partial S|\leq|S|+|\partial S|\leq|\supp(v-\widetilde{v})|+|\supp(w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v})|\leq n^{a}+2n^{a}=3n^{a},

where the first two steps are the same as previous inequality, and the third step follows from Col. 2 and Col. 5 of Table
12
.

For the fifth part (
Query
) of Col. 6, we have

\displaystyle|S\cup\partial S|\leq|S|+|\partial S|\leq|\supp(v-\widetilde{v})|+|\supp(w^{\appr}-\widetilde{v})|\leq n^{a}+n^{\widetilde{a}}\leq 2n^{a},

where the first two steps are the same as previous inequality, the third step follows from Col. 2 and Col. 5 of Table
12
.
∎

E.2
Running time of
Query

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.3
. We will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in Lemma
E.1
.

Fact E.2

(Sparsity guarantees for
Query
)
.

When entering
Query
we have the following sparsity guarantee (from Table
13
):

1.

\|\Gamma+\partial\Gamma\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

2.

\|\partial\Gamma\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
,

3.

\|\Gamma\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

4.

\|\xi+\partial\xi\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

5.

\|\partial\xi\|_{0}\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
,

6.

|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
,

7.

|S|\leq n^{a}
.

8.

9.

Lemma E.3

(Running time of
Query
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes

1.

O(n^{1+b}+n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time to compute

\displaystyle r_{2}\leftarrow Q[j]\xi+R[j]\gamma_{2}+R[j]\partial\Gamma M(\xi+\partial\xi)+\big(Q[j]+R[j]\Gamma M\big)\partial\xi,

2.

O(n^{1+b})
time to compute

r_{3}\leftarrow R[j](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)\beta_{2},

3.

O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time to compute

\partial\gamma\leftarrow B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(\beta_{2})_{\partial S\backslash S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]\cdot(\xi+\partial\xi)+E\cdot\partial\xi,

4.

O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time to compute

(U^{\prime},C,U)\leftarrow\textsc{Decompose}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}]\Big),

5.

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time to compute

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow

\displaystyle E_{\partial S}-B_{(\partial S\backslash S)}M_{(\partial S\backslash S),\partial S},\penalty\ E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}}\leftarrow-E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}},\penalty\ E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{(S\cap\partial S)\backslash S^{\prime}}\leftarrow 0,\text{\penalty\ and}

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow

\displaystyle[B_{\partial S},B_{\partial S},E^{\mathrm{tmp}}],

6.

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time to compute

\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow U^{\mathrm{tmp}}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}U^{\mathrm{tmp}})^{-1}U^{\top}\cdot(\gamma_{1}+\partial\gamma),

7.

O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a}+n^{1+b})
time to compute

r_{4}\leftarrow\Big(\mathcal{L}_{c}[(Q[j])_{S^{\new}}]+F[j]+R[j]\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R[j]\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]\Big)\cdot(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma).

Overall, the time to compute
r
(which is
r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4}
) is

\displaystyle O(n^{1+b}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})).

Claim E.4

(Part 1 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O(n^{1+b}+n^{a+\widetilde{a}})

time to compute

\displaystyle r_{2}\leftarrow Q[j]\xi+R[j]\gamma_{2}+\underbrace{R[j]\partial\Gamma M(\xi+\partial\xi)}_{a_{1}}+\underbrace{\big(Q[j]+R[j]\Gamma M\big)\partial\xi}_{a_{2}}.

Proof.

The running time of this step can be split into the following parts:

The first part is to compute
Q[j]\xi
by multiplying a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
Q[j]
with a
n\times 1
vector
\xi
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time. The second part is to compute
R[j]\gamma_{2}
by multiplying a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
with a
n\times 1
vector
\gamma_{2}
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

The third part is to compute
a_{1}
as follows:

1.

We multiply a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\partial\Gamma
(Part
2
of Fact
E.2
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
(\xi+\partial\xi)
(Part
4
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

2.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
and a
n\times 1
vector
(\partial\Gamma M(\xi+\partial\xi)).

It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

So computing
a_{1}
takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}}+n^{1+b})
time in total.

The fourth part is to compute
a_{2}
as follows:

1.

We multiply a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma
(Part
3
of Fact
E.2
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\partial\xi
(Part
5
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

2.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
with a
n\times 1
vector
(\Gamma M\partial\xi)
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

3.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
Q[j]
and a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\partial\xi
(Part
5
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{\widetilde{a}+b})
time.

So computing
a_{2}
takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}}+n^{1+b})
time in total since
\widetilde{a}\leq 1
.

Thus the total running time is
O(n^{1+b}+n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
.
∎

Claim E.5

(Part 2 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O(n^{1+b})
time to compute
r_{3}\leftarrow R[j](\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)\beta_{2}
.

Proof.

The running time of this step can be split into the following parts.

1.

We multiply a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)
(Part
1
of Fact
E.2
) and a
n\times 1
vector
\beta_{2}
. It takes
O(n^{a})
time.

2.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
and a
n\times 1
vector
((\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)\beta_{2})
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

The total running time is

O(n^{a}+n^{1+b})=O(n^{1+b})
since
a\leq 1
.
∎

Claim E.6

(Part 3 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time to compute
\partial\gamma\leftarrow B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(\beta_{2})_{\partial S\backslash S}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]\cdot(\xi+\partial\xi)+E\cdot\partial\xi
.

Proof.

The running time of this step can be split into the following parts.

1.

We multiply a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(\beta_{2})_{\partial S\backslash S}]
(since
|\partial S\backslash S|\leq|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
by Part
6
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

2.

We multiply a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]
that only has
n^{\widetilde{a}}
non-zero rows (since
|\partial S\backslash S|\leq|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
by Part
6
of Fact
E.2
) with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
(\xi+\partial\xi)
(Part
4
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

3.

We multiply a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}](\xi+\partial\xi))
(since
|\partial S\backslash S|\leq|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
by Part
6
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

4.

We multiply a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
E
with a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse vector
\partial\xi
(Part
5
of Fact
E.2
). It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

Thus the total running time is
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
.
∎

Claim E.7

(Part 4 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time to compute

(U^{\prime},C,U)\leftarrow\textsc{Decompose}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}]\Big).

And the size of the computed matrices are
U^{\prime},U\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{a}\times c}
,
C\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times c}
, where
c\leq O(n^{\widetilde{a}})
.

Proof.

For ease of notation, we denote
N:=\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}]
.

From Lemma
C.5
we know that the non-zero entries of
N
can be split into three parts:
N_{\partial S,(S\backslash\partial S)}
,
N_{(S\backslash\partial S),\partial S}
, and
N_{\partial S,\partial S}
. Thus we don’t need to compute
N
explicitly, but only compute the non-zero entries of
N
, which takes
O(|\partial S|\cdot|S\backslash\partial S|+|\partial S|\cdot|\partial S|)=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time (from Part
7
and Part
6
of Fact
E.2
and since
\widetilde{a}\leq a
).

Then
N
satisfies the requirement of Lemma
C.4
with
S_{1}=S\backslash\partial S
,
S_{2}=\partial S
, so the function
Decompose
can be computed in
O(n^{a}|S_{2}|)=O(n^{a}|\partial S|)=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
). Also using Lemma
C.4
, we know that the computed matrix
C
has size
c\times c=(3|S_{2}|)\times(3|S_{2}|)=(3|\partial S|)\times(3|\partial S|)
, thus
c\leq O(n^{\widetilde{a}})
(Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

Thus the total running time is
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
.
∎

Claim E.8

(Part 5 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time to compute

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow E_{\partial S}-B_{(\partial S\cap S)}M_{(\partial S\cap S),\partial S};\penalty\ \penalty\ E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}}\leftarrow-E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}};\penalty\ \penalty\ E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{(S\cap\partial S)\backslash S^{\prime}}\leftarrow 0;\penalty\ \penalty\ U^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow[B_{\partial S},B_{\partial S},E^{\mathrm{tmp}}].

Proof.

To compute the initial
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow E_{\partial S}-B_{(\partial S\cap S)}M_{(\partial S\cap S),\partial S}
, we need to multiply a
6n^{a}\times|\partial S\cap S|
matrix
B_{(\partial S\cap S)}
with a
|\partial S\cap S|\times|\partial S|
matrix
M_{(\partial S\cap S),\partial S}
, and this takes
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(6n^{a},|\partial S\cap S|,|\partial S|)=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

Finally note that the other two steps
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}}\leftarrow-E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{S^{\prime}}
and
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{(S\cap\partial S)\backslash S^{\prime}}\leftarrow 0
to adjust
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes the same time as the size of
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, which is
n^{a}\times|\partial S|=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
(Part
6
of Fact
E.2
). Computing
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
only needs to copy entries from the already computed
B
and
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, so it also takes the same time as the size of
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, which is
n^{a}\times 3|\partial S|=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
. Thus the total running time is

\displaystyle O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}})+n^{a+\widetilde{a}})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}})).

∎

Claim E.9

(Part 6 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow U^{\mathrm{tmp}}\underbrace{(C^{-1}+U^{\top}U^{\mathrm{tmp}})^{-1}}_{N}U^{\top}\cdot(\gamma_{1}+\partial\gamma).

Proof.

First note that from Lemma
E.7
we have that the sizes of
U^{\prime}
and
U
are
6n^{a}\times 3|\partial S|
, and the size of
C
is
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
. From the assignment of
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
on Line
15
, we know that the size of
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is also
6n^{a}\times 3|\partial S|
. The running time of this step can be split into the following parts:

The first part is to compute the
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
N
.

1.

Computing the inverse of a
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
C
takes
O(n^{\widetilde{a}\omega})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

2.

We multiply a
3|\partial S|\times 6n^{a}
rectangular matrix
U^{\top}
with a
6n^{a}\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, which takes
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(3|\partial S|,6n^{a},3|\partial S|)=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
time (
|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
from Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

3.

We add a
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
C^{-1}
with a
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
U^{\top}U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
and calculate its inverse. It takes
O(|\partial S|^{\omega})=O(n^{\widetilde{a}\omega})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

Thus in total computing
N
takes time
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})+n^{\widetilde{a}\omega})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
, sinnce
\widetilde{a}\leq a
and thus
n^{\widetilde{a}\omega}={\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{\widetilde{a}})\leq{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})
.

The second part is to compute the
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}
.

1.

We multiply the
3|\partial S|\times 6n^{a}
matrix
U^{\top}
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\gamma_{1}+\partial\gamma)
. This takes
O(n^{a}\cdot|\partial S|)=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

2.

We multiply the
3|\partial S|\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
N
with a
3|\partial S|\times 1
vector
U^{\top}(\gamma_{1}+\partial\gamma)
. This takes
O(|\partial S|^{2})=O(n^{2\widetilde{a}})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

3.

We multiply the
6n^{a}\times 3|\partial S|
matrix
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
3|\partial S|\times 1
vector
NU^{\top}(\gamma_{1}+\partial\gamma)
. This takes
O(n^{a}\cdot|\partial S|)=O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time (Part
6
of Fact
E.2
).

Thus this part takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time since
\widetilde{a}\leq a
.

Thus the total time to compute
\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})+n^{a+\widetilde{a}})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})).

∎

Claim E.10

(Part 7 of Lemma
E.3
)
.

In procedure
Query
(Algorithm
12
), it takes
O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a}+n^{1+b})
time to compute

\displaystyle r_{4}\leftarrow\Big(\mathcal{L}_{c}[(Q[j])_{S^{\new}}]+F[j]+R[j]\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R[j]\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]\Big)\cdot(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma).

Proof.

The running time can be split into the following parts:

1.

We multiply a
n^{\widetilde{a}}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\partial\Gamma
(Part
2
of Fact
E.2
) with a
n\times 6n^{a}
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma)
. It takes
O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a})
time.

2.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
with a
n\times 1
vector
(\partial\Gamma\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}](\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma))
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

3.

We multiply a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma
(Part
3
of Fact
E.2
) with a
n\times 6n^{a}
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]
that only has
n^{\widetilde{a}}
non-zero columns (since
|\partial S\backslash S|\leq|\partial S|\leq n^{\widetilde{a}}
by Part
6
of Fact
E.2
) and then with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma)
. It takes
O(n^{a+\widetilde{a}})
time.

4.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times n
matrix
R[j]
with a
n\times 1
vector
(\Gamma(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma))
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

5.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
F[j]
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma)
. It takes
O(n^{1+b})
time.

6.

We multiply a
n^{b}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{c}[(Q[j])_{S^{\new}}]
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
(\gamma^{\mathrm{tmp}}-\gamma_{1}-\partial\gamma)
. It takes
O(n^{b+a})
time.

Thus this part takes
O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a}+n^{1+b}+n^{b+a})=O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a}+n^{1+b})
time, since
a\leq 1
.

Thus, the total running time to compute
r_{4}
is
O(n^{\widetilde{a}+a}+n^{1+b})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

Summing over the time to compute
r_{2}
,
r_{3}
,
(U^{\prime},C,U)
,
\partial\gamma
,
r_{4,1}
,
r_{4,2}
,
r_{4,3}
,
U^{\mathrm{tmp}}
, and
r_{4,4}
, the total running time of computing
r
is

\displaystyle O(n^{1+b}+n^{a+\widetilde{a}}+n^{a+b}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))=O(n^{1+b}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}})),

which follows by
a\leq 1
and
n^{a+\widetilde{a}}\leq{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a},n^{\widetilde{a}}))
.
∎

E.3
Running time of
MatrixUpdate

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.12
.
We will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in Lemma
E.1
.

Fact E.11

(Sparsity guarantees for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

When entering
MatrixUpdate
we have the following sparsity guarantee (from Table
13
):

1.

|S^{\new}|\leq|S\cup\partial S|\leq 3k
,

2.

\|\Gamma^{\new}\|_{0}=\|\Gamma+\partial\Gamma\|_{0}=k
.

Lemma E.12

(Running time of
MatrixUpdate
)
.

In the procedure
MatrixUpdate
(in Algorithm
13
) it takes

1.

O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,n))
time to compute

\displaystyle M^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow M-M_{S^{\new}}\cdot((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top},

2.

O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
time to compute

\displaystyle Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow Q+R(\Gamma^{\new}M^{\mathrm{tmp}})+R\sqrt{V}(M^{\mathrm{tmp}}-M),

3.

O(n^{2+o(1)})
time to compute

\displaystyle\beta_{1}\leftarrow Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}),

4.

O(n^{2})
time to compute

\displaystyle\beta_{2}\leftarrow M^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}).

Further, it takes
O(n)
time to do all other computation. So the overall running time of the procedure
MatrixUpdate
is
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
.

Claim E.13

(Part 1 of Lemma
E.12
)
.

In procedure
MatrixUpdate
, it takes
O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,n))
time to compute

M^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow M-M_{S^{\new}}\cdot((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}.

Proof.

The running time of computing
M^{\new}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Computing the inverse of a
O(k)\times O(k)
matrix
((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})
(the size follows from
|S^{\new}|=O(k)
, see Part
1
of Fact
E.11
), this part takes
O(k^{\omega+o(1)})
time.

2.

Computing the multiplication of a
n\times O(k)
matrix
M_{S^{\new}}
with a
O(k)\times O(k)
matrix
((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}
, this part takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,k))
time.

3.

Computing the multiplication of a
n\times O(k)
matrix
M_{S^{\new}}((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}
with a
O(k)\times n
matrix
(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}
, this part takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,n))
time.

4.

Subtracting a
n\times n
matrix
M_{S^{\new}}\cdot((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}
from the
n\times n
matrix
M
, this part takes
O(n^{2})
time.

So in total computing
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes time

O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,k)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,n)+n^{2})=O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,k,n)).

∎

Claim E.14

(Part 2 of Lemma
E.12
)
.

In
MatrixUpdate
, it takes
O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
time to compute

Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow Q+\underbrace{R(\Gamma^{\new}M^{\mathrm{tmp}})}_{N_{1}}+\underbrace{R\sqrt{V}(M^{\mathrm{tmp}}-M)}_{N_{2}}.

Proof.

The running time of computing
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

The first part is to compute the
n\times n
matrix
N_{1}
:

1.

Multiplying a
k
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma^{\new}
(Part
2
of Fact
E.11
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes
O(kn)
time.

2.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
k
-row-sparse
n\times n
matrix
(\Gamma^{\new}M^{\mathrm{tmp}})
(Part
1
of Fact) takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
time.

So in total computing
N_{1}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
time.

The second part is to compute the
n\times n
matrix
N_{2}
. By the definition of
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
we have

\displaystyle N_{2}=R\sqrt{V}(M^{\mathrm{tmp}}-M)=R\sqrt{V}M_{S^{\new}}\cdot\underbrace{((\Delta_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}^{\new})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}}_{N_{3}}.

And we computes
N_{2}
as follows:

1.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
R
with a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{V}
takes
O(n^{2+o(1)})
time.

2.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
R\sqrt{V}
with a
n\times O(k)
matrix
M_{S^{\new}}
(Part
1
of Fact
E.11
) takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n,k))
time.

3.

The
O(k)\times n
matrix
N_{3}
is already computed when we were computing
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
(Claim
E.13
), and this takes
O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(k,k,n))
time.

4.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times O(k)
matrix
(R\sqrt{V}M_{S^{\new}})
with a
O(k)\times n
matrix
N_{3}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
time.

So in total computing
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes time

\displaystyle O(n^{2+o(1)}+k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(k,k,n)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))=O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n)).

∎

Claim E.15

(Part 3 of Lemma
E.12
)
.

In
MatrixUpdate
, it takes
O(n^{2+o(1)})
time to compute

\beta_{1}\leftarrow Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}).

Proof.

To compute
\beta_{1}
, we first multiply a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{W^{\appr}}
, which takes
O(n^{2+o(1)})
time. Then we multiply a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
Q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}
with a
n\times 1
vector, which takes
O(n^{2+o(1)})
time. So in total computing
\beta_{1}
takes time
O(n^{2+o(1)})
.
∎

Claim E.16

(Part 4 of Lemma
E.12
)
.

In procedure
MatrixUpdate
, it takes
O(n^{2})
time to compute

\beta_{2}\leftarrow M^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(h^{\appr}).

Proof.

To compute
\beta_{2}
, we first multiply a
n\times n
matrix
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{W^{\appr}}
, which takes
O(n^{2})
time. Then we multiply a
n^{1}\times n
matrix
M^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{W^{\appr}}
with a
n\times 1
vector, which takes
O(n^{2})
time. So in total computing
\beta_{1}
takes time
O(n^{2})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.12
.

Overall time.

The running time of procedure
MatrixUpdate
is

O(k^{\omega+o(1)}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n)+n^{2+o(1)})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},k,n))
.
∎

E.4
Running time of
PartialMatrixUpdate

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.18
.
We will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in Lemma
E.1
.

Fact E.17

(Sparsity guarantees for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

When entering
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from Table
12
and Table
13
):

1.

\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

2.

\|\partial\Gamma\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
,

3.

\|\Gamma+\partial\Gamma\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

4.

\|f(\widetilde{g})-f(g)\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

5.

\|\xi\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

6.

|S|\leq n^{a}
,

7.

|\partial S|\leq\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
.

8.

9.

Lemma E.18

(Running time of
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
) it takes

1.

O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle(U^{\prime},C,U)\leftarrow\textsc{Decompose}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}]\Big),

2.

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a}))
time to compute

\displaystyle B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}B,

3.

O(n)
time to compute

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g),

4.

O(n^{2a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S^{\new}}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}},

5.

O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{2}+(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})f(\widetilde{g})+\partial\Gamma M(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)).

6.

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time to compute

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow F+R\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}].

7.

O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time to compute

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{tmp}}=E+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])-BU^{\prime}(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}U^{\top}E.

Further, it takes
O(n)
time to do all other computation. So the overall running time of the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
is
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
.

Claim E.19

(Part 1 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle(U^{\prime},C,U)\leftarrow\textsc{Decompose}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{*}[(\Delta^{\new}_{S^{\new},S^{\new}})^{-1}+M_{S^{\new},S^{\new}}]-\mathcal{L}_{*}[\Delta_{S,S}^{-1}+M_{S,S}]\Big).

And the size of the computed matrices are
U^{\prime},U\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times c}
,
C\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times c}
, where
c=O(\widetilde{k})
.

Proof.

The analysis for the
Decompose
function in
PartialMatrixUpdate
is the same as the analysis in
Query
(Claim
E.7
). We still have
|S|\leq n^{a}
(Part
6
of Fact
E.17
), but the bound on
\partial S
is different and now we have
|\partial S|=\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
(Part
7
of Fact
E.17
). So
c=O(|\partial S|)=O(\widetilde{k})
.

And to compute
(U^{\prime},C,U)
it takes
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time.
∎

Claim E.20

(Part 2 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a}))
time to compute

\displaystyle B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B-BU^{\prime}\underbrace{(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}}_{N}U^{\top}B.

Proof.

The running time of computing
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}}
can be split into the following parts.

The first part is to compute
N\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times c}
as follows:

1.

Multiplying a
c\times 6n^{a}
matrix
U^{\top}
with a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n^{a}))
time.

2.

Multiplying a
c\times 6n^{a}
matrix
(U^{\top}B)
with a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
U^{\prime}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},c))
time.

3.

Computing the inverse of a
c\times c
matrix
(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})
takes
O(c^{\omega+o(1)})
time.

So the total running time to compute
N
is
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n^{a})+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},c)+c^{\omega+o(1)})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a}))
(since
c=O(\widetilde{k})\leq O(n^{a})
by Claim
E.19
), and
\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
.

The second part is to compute
BU^{\prime}NU^{\top}B\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}}
as follows:

1.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
U^{\prime}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},c))
time.

2.

Multiplying a
c\times 6n^{a}
matrix
U^{\top}
with a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n^{a}))
time.

3.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
BU^{\prime}
with a
c\times c
matrix
N
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,c))
time.

4.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
BU^{\prime}N
with a
c\times 6n^{a}
matrix
U^{\top}B
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,n^{a}))
time.

So the total running time to compute
BU^{\prime}NU^{\top}B
is
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},c)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,c))=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
(since
c=O(\widetilde{k})\leq O(n^{a})
by Claim
E.19
), and
\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
.

Finally, subtracting
BU^{\prime}NU^{\top}B
from
B
takes
O(n^{2a})
time since both matrices has size
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
.

So in total computing
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}}
takes time
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a})+n^{2a})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a}))
.
∎

Claim E.21

(Part 3 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O(n)
time to compute
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)
. And the computed vector
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 1}
is
n^{a}
-sparse.

Proof.

The
O(n)
running time follows trivially from the fact that
\sqrt{W^{\appr}}
and
\sqrt{V}
are
n\times n
diagonal matrices, and
f(\widetilde{g})
and
f(g)
are
n\times 1
vectors. We also have

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\|_{0}=\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)\|_{0}=\max\{\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0},\|f(\widetilde{g})-f(g)\|_{0}\}\leq n^{a},

which follows from Part
1
and
4
of Fact
E.17
.
∎

Claim E.22

(Part 4 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O(n^{2a})
time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S^{\new}}]+B^{\mathrm{tmp}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
.

Proof.

The running time of computing
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S^{\new}}]
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

2.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]
with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
(Claim
E.21
) takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

3.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\new}})^{\top}]\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

So in total computing
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.
∎

Claim E.23

(Part 5 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{2}+(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)M\underbrace{(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})f(\widetilde{g})}_{a_{1}}+\partial\Gamma M\underbrace{(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g))}_{a_{2}}.

Proof.

First note that the
n\times 1
vectors
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
can both be computed in
O(n)
time. Also,

\displaystyle\|a_{1}\|_{0}=\|(\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}})f(\widetilde{g})\|_{0}\leq\min\{\|\sqrt{W^{\appr}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\|_{0},\|f(\widetilde{g})\|_{0}\}\leq\widetilde{k},

where the last step follows from Part
1
of Fact
E.17
. And

\displaystyle\|a_{2}\|_{0}=\|\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(\widetilde{g})-\sqrt{V}f(g)\|_{0}=\|\xi\|_{0}\leq n^{a},

where the last step follows from Part
5
of Fact
E.17
.

The running time of computing
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
(\Gamma+\partial\Gamma)
(Part
3
of Fact
E.17
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
\widetilde{k}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
a_{1}
takes
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time.

2.

Multiplying a
\widetilde{k}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\partial\Gamma
(Part
2
of Fact
E.17
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
a_{2}
takes
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
time.

So in total computing
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes time
O(\widetilde{k}n^{a})
.
∎

Claim E.24

(Part 6 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time to compute

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow F+R\Gamma\cdot(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])+R\partial\Gamma\cdot\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}].

Proof.

By sparsity guarantee(Part
7
,
6
of Fact
E.17
), we have
|\partial S|+|S^{\prime}|\leq|\partial S|\leq\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
.
The running time of computing
F^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
R
with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma
and then with a
\widetilde{k}
-column-sparse
n\times 6n^{a}
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}]
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time.

2.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
R
with a
\widetilde{k}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\partial\Gamma
and then with a
n\times 6n^{a}
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},\widetilde{k},n^{a})
time.

3.

Adding two matrices
R\Gamma(\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{\partial S\backslash S}]-\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\prime}}])
,
R\partial\Gamma\mathcal{L}_{c}[M_{S^{\new}}]
on the current stored matrix
F
takes
O(n^{1+o(1)+a})
time.

So in total computing
F^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k})+n^{1+o(1)+a})=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time since
O(n^{1+o(1)+a})\leq O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1+o(1)},n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
.
∎

Claim E.25

(Part 7 of Lemma
E.18
)
.

In the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
), it takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k}))
time to compute

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow E+\underbrace{B^{\mathrm{tmp}}(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])}_{N_{2}}-\underbrace{BU^{\prime}\underbrace{(C^{-1}+U^{\top}BU^{\prime})^{-1}}_{N}U^{\top}E}_{N_{1}}

Proof.

By sparsity guarantee(Part
7
,
6
of Fact
E.17
), we have
|\partial S|\leq\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
,
|S|\leq n^{a}
. So
|S^{\new}|\leq|S\cup\partial S|\leq|S|+|\partial S|\leq 3n^{a}
.
The running time of computing
F^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

First we compute
N_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times n}
.

1.

We already compute the time of computing
N
in Claim
E.20
. It takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n^{a}))
time.

2.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
U^{\prime}
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},c))
time.

3.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
BU^{\prime}
with a
c\times c
matrix
N
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,c))
time.

4.

Multiplying a
c\times 6n^{a}
matrix
U^{\top}
with a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
E
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n))
time.

5.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times c
matrix
BU^{\prime}N
with a
c\times n
matrix
U^{\top}E
takes time
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,n))
.

Next, we compute
N_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{6n^{a}\times n}
. Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B^{\mathrm{tmp}}
with a
\widetilde{k}
-row-sparse
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
(\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{\partial S\backslash S})^{\top}]-\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S^{\prime}})^{\top}])
takes
O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},\widetilde{k},n)
time.

So in total computing
E^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes time

\displaystyle\penalty\ O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n^{a})+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},c)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,c)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^{a},n)+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,n))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},c))=\penalty\ O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k})),

where the first step follows since
c=O(\widetilde{k})\leq O(n^{a})
by Claim
E.19
), and
\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.18
.

Overall time.
So in total the running time of procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
14
) is

\displaystyle O(\widetilde{k}n^{a}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^{a},n^{a})+n+n^{2a}+{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k}))=O({\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k})).

∎

E.5
Running time of
VectorUpdate

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.27
. We will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in Lemma
E.1
.

Fact E.26

(Sparsity guarantees for
VectorUpdate
)
.

When entering
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from Table
12
and Table
13
):

1.

\|\Gamma\|_{0}=\|\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

2.

\|f(h^{\appr})-f(g)\|_{0}=p
.

Lemma E.27

(Running time of
VectorUpdate
)
.

The procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
) takes

1.

O(pn^{1+o(1)})
time to compute

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\beta_{1}+Q\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g)),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\beta_{2}+M\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g)),

2.

O(n)
time to compute

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})f(h^{\appr}),

3.

O(n^{2a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}^{\mathrm{tmp}}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}},

4.

O(n^{2a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\Gamma M\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}.

Overall, the running time of procedure
VectorUpdate
is

\displaystyle O(n^{2a}+pn^{1+o(1)}).

Claim E.28

(Part 1 of Lemma
E.27
)
.

In the procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
), it takes
O(pn^{1+o(1)})
time to compute

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\beta_{1}+Q\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g)),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\beta_{2}+M\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g)).

Proof.

We compute
\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
as follows.

1.

Multiplying a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{V}
with a
p
-sparse vector
f(h^{\appr})-f(g)
(Part
2
of Fact
E.26
) takes
O(p)
time, and the resulting vector
\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g))
is also
p
-sparse.

2.

Multiplying a
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix
Q
with a
p
-sparse vector
\sqrt{V}(f(h^{\appr})-f(g))
takes
O(pn^{1+o(1)})
time.

So the total running time to compute
\beta_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
O(pn^{1+o(1)})
.

Following the same reason and note that
M
has size
n\times n
, we can compute
\beta_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
in
O(pn)\leq O(pn^{1+o(1)})
time.
∎

Claim E.29

(Part 2 of Lemma
E.27
)
.

In the procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
), it takes
O(n)
time to compute
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})f(h^{\appr})
.
And the computed
n\times 1
vector
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
n^{a}
-sparse.

Proof.

\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is computed by multiplying a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})
with a
n\times 1
vector
f(h^{\appr})
, and this takes
O(n)
time. We also have

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\|_{0}=\|(\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V})f(h^{\appr})\|_{0}\leq\min\{\|\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}-\sqrt{V}\|_{0},\|f(h^{\appr})\|_{0}\}\leq n^{a},

where the last step follows from Part
1
of Fact
E.26
.
∎

Claim E.30

(Part 3 of Lemma
E.27
)
.

In the procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
), it takes
O(n^{2a})
time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}^{\mathrm{tmp}}]+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
.

Proof.

The running time of computing
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[\beta_{2,S}^{\mathrm{tmp}}]
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

2.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]
with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
(Claim
E.29
) takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

3.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

The total running time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
O(n^{2a})
.
∎

Claim E.31

(Part 4 of Lemma
E.27
)
.

In the procedure
VectorUpdate
(Algorithm
15
), it takes
O(n^{2a})
time to compute
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\Gamma M\cdot\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
.

Proof.

We compute
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
by multiplying a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma
(Part
1
of Fact
E.26
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
((Claim
E.29
)), which takes
O(n^{2a})
time.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.27
.

Overall, the total running time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
O(pn^{1+o(1)}+n^{2a})=O(pn^{1+o(1)})
, since we always have
p\geq n^{a}
.
∎

E.6
Running time of
PartialVectorUpdate

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.33
. We will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in Lemma
E.1
.

Fact E.32

(Sparsity guarantees for
PartialVectorUpdate
)
.

When entering
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from Table
12
and Table
13
):

1.

\|\Gamma\|_{0}\leq n^{a}
,

2.

\|f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\|_{0}=\widetilde{p}\leq 2n^{a}
.

Lemma E.33

(Running time of
PartialVectorUpdate
)
.

In the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(in Algorithm
16
) it takes

1.

O(n)
time to compute

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g),

2.

O(n^{2a}+\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{1}+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big),

3.

O(\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time to compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{2}+\Gamma M\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big).

Overall, the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
takes
O(n^{2a}+\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time.

Claim E.34

(Part 1 of Lemma
E.33
)
.

In the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
), it takes
O(n)
time to compute
\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}f(h^{\appr})-\sqrt{V}f(g)
.

Proof.

\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is computed by multiplying the
n\times n
diagonal matrices
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}
and
\sqrt{V}
with the
n\times 1
vectors
f(h^{\appr})
and
f(g)
respectively, and this takes
O(n)
time.
∎

Claim E.35

(Part 2 of Lemma
E.33
)
.

In the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
), it takes
O(n^{2a}+\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{1}+B\cdot\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\cdot\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)
.

Proof.

The running time of computing
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}
with a
\widetilde{p}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
(Part
2
of Fact
E.32
) takes
O(\widetilde{p})
time, and the resulting vector
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
is also
\widetilde{p}
-sparse.

2.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times n
matrix
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]
with a
\widetilde{p}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
takes
O(\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time.

3.

Multiplying a
6n^{a}\times 6n^{a}
matrix
B
with a
6n^{a}\times 1
vector
\mathcal{L}_{r}[(M_{S})^{\top}]\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
takes
O(n^{2a})
time.

4.

Adding two
n\times 1
vectors together takes
O(n)
time.

So in total the running time to compute
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
O(n^{2a}+\widetilde{p}n^{a})
.
∎

Claim E.36

(Part 3 of Lemma
E.33
)
.

In the procedure
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
), it takes
O(\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time to compute
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\leftarrow\gamma_{2}+\Gamma M\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}\big(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g})\big)
.

Proof.

The running time of computing
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
can be split into the following parts:

1.

Multiplying a
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}
with a
\widetilde{p}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
(Part
2
of Fact
E.32
) takes
O(\widetilde{p})
time, and the resulting vector
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
is also
\widetilde{p}
-sparse.

2.

Multiplying a
n^{a}
-sparse
n\times n
diagonal matrix
\Gamma
(Part
1
of Fact
E.32
) with a
n\times n
matrix
M
and then with a
\widetilde{p}
-sparse
n\times 1
vector
\sqrt{\widetilde{V}}(f(h^{\appr})-f(\widetilde{g}))
takes
O(\widetilde{p}n^{a})
time.

3.

Adding two
n\times 1
vectors together takes
O(n)
time.

So in total the running time to compute
\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
is
O(\widetilde{p}n^{a})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.33
.

Overall, the running time of
PartialVectorUpdate
(Algorithm
16
) is
O(n^{2a}+\widetilde{p}n^{a})
.
∎

E.7
Running time of
Initialize

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
E.37
.

Lemma E.37

(Running time of
Initialize
)
.

The procedure
Initialize
(in Algorithm
5
) takes
O(n^{\omega+o(1)})
time.

Proof.

The bottleneck in
Initialize
is to compute
M=A^{\top}(AVA^{\top})^{-1}A
and
Q=R\sqrt{V}M
. Other operations take at most
O(n^{2+o(1)})
time.

Computing
M
involves matrix multiplication of two
n\times n
matrix, and inversion of a
n\times n
matrix. Both of them can be done in
O(n^{\omega+o(1)})
time.

Computing
Q
involves matrix multiplication of
n^{1+o(1)}\times n
matrix with a
n\times n
matrix. This can be done in
O(n^{\omega+o(1)})
time.

Therefore, the total running time is
O(n^{\omega+o(1)})
.
∎

Appendix F
Data structure : amortized time

In this section we provide an amortized analysis of the four procedures
MatrixUpdate
,
PartialMatrixUpdate
,
VectorUpdate
, and
PartialVectorUpdate
. Our amortized analysis builds upon the amortized analysis of
[
CLS19
]
.

F.1
Definitions and Preliminaries

Our algorithm makes
T
calls to the procedure
UpdateQuery
. For clarity, we define some notations with superscripts that represent the number of iterations. For the input diagonal matrix
W
and its approximations
V
and
\widetilde{V}
, we define the following notations:

Definition F.1

(Definitions of sequences
\{w^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{v^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
and
\{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
)
.

When initializing, we use
v^{(0)}
and
\widetilde{v}^{(0)}
to denote the initial values of the data structure members
v
and
\widetilde{v}
. Note that
v^{(0)}=\widetilde{v}^{(0)}=w^{(0)}
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we use
w^{(j+1)}
to denote the input
w^{\new}
of
UpdateQuery
. Since the procedure
Update
might update both
v
and
\widetilde{v}
, we use
v^{(j+1)}
and
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}
to denote the new values of
v
and
\widetilde{v}
respectively.

We define similar notations for the input query vector
h
and its approximations
g
and
\widetilde{g}
:

Definition F.2

(Definitions of sequences
\{h^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{g^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
and
\{\widetilde{g}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
)
.

When initializing, we use
g^{(0)}
and
\widetilde{g}^{(0)}
to denote the initial values of the data structure members
g
and
\widetilde{g}
. Note that
g^{(0)}=\widetilde{g}^{(0)}=h^{(0)}
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we use
h^{(j+1)}
to denote the input
h^{\new}
of
UpdateQuery
. Since the procedure
Update
might update both
g
and
\widetilde{g}
, we use
g^{(j+1)}
and
\widetilde{g}^{(j+1)}
to denote the new values of
g
and
\widetilde{g}
respectively.

The following four notations will be helpful to prove the amortized cost of the procedures:

Definition F.3

(Definition of
k
,
\widetilde{k}
,
p
, and
\widetilde{p}
)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we define the following notations:

1.

k_{j}
and
\widetilde{k}_{j}
denote outputs
k
and
\widetilde{k}
of
UpdateV
on Line
4
of
UpdateQuery
(Algorithm
8
).

2.

p_{j}
and
\widetilde{p}_{j}
denote outputs
p
and
\widetilde{p}
of
UpdateG
on Line
5
of
UpdateQuery
(Algorithm
8
),

We also define a function
\psi
that approximates absolute function
|x|
around
0
. It will be used to define the potential functions for amortized analysis.

Definition F.4

(Definition of
\psi
function)
.

Let
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\in(0,1/10)
.
Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be defined by

\displaystyle\psi(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{|x|^{2}}{2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}},&|x|\in[0,\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}];\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}-\frac{(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}-|x|)^{2}}{2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}},&|x|\in(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}];\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},&|x|\in(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},+\infty).\end{cases}

We also make the following assumption about the values of
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}
:

Assumption F.5
.

The error parameters satisfy
0<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}<1/10
and
0<\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}<\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{100\log n}
.

F.2
Facts based on
Adjust
and two level of
SoftThreshold

The following facts are direct results from the algorithms, and they are proved solely based on the algorithms. They will be useful when proving the lemmas of amortized running time.

Fact F.6

(Characterization of
\widetilde{k}
(Line
4
),
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
(Line
4
),
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
(Line
5
) of
UpdateV
)
.

In the
j
-th iteration,
\forall i\in[n]
, let
y_{i}=|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1|
. Let
\pi:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting such that
y_{\pi(i)}\geq y_{\pi(i+1)}
. We have the following guarantees from the description of procedures
SoftThreshold
(Algorithm
7
) and
Adjust
(Algorithm
6
).

\displaystyle 1.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \{i\in[n]:\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2\}\subseteq\pi([\widetilde{k}])

\displaystyle 2.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{i}=\begin{cases}w^{(j+1)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}]);\\
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle 3.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\begin{cases}v^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
w^{(j+1)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ but\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\notin[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle 4.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \forall i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}]),\penalty\ |w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}-1|<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}.

Proof.

Part 1 and 2.

In Line
4
in Procedure
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
), we create
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}},\widetilde{k}
by calling procedure
\textsc{SoftThreshold}(y_{i}\leftarrow\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),w^{(j+1)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)},\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2,n^{\widetilde{a}})
.

From the initial assignment of
\widetilde{k}
on Line
5
of
SoftThreshold
(Algorithm
7
), and the fact that the repeat-loop (Line
7
to
9
) only increases
k
, we know that for any
i
such that
y_{i}=\psi((w^{\new}_{i}-\widetilde{v}_{i})/\widetilde{v}_{i})\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2
,
i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])
.

By how we calculate
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}
in
SoftThreshold
(Line
11
of Algorithm
7
), Part 2 follows directly.

Part 3.

We get
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
by calling procedure
\textsc{Adjust}\left(\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}},\widetilde{v}^{(j)},v^{(j)},\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}\right)
(Line
5
in Procedure
UpdateV
, Algorithm
9
). By Part 2
and the rule of how we calculate
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}
(see Line
5
to
8
in Procedure
Adjust
(Algorithm
6
)):

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}\leftarrow\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{i}

\displaystyle\textbf{if}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \widetilde{v}_{i}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\neq\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \widetilde{v}_{i}^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}]\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \textbf{then}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \widetilde{v}_{i}^{\new}\leftarrow v^{(j)}_{i}

It is easy to see that for
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}])
,
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
, so
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
. And for
i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])
, if
w^{(j+1)}_{i}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}]
, then
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}
is adjusted to be
v^{(j)}_{i}
, otherwise
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}_{i}=w^{(j+1)}_{i}
.

Part 4.
From Part 3,
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}
has three possible values. If
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=w^{(j+1)}_{i}
, we have
|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}-1|=0
.

If
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}
, we have
w_{i}^{(j+1)}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}]
. So
|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}-1|<\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
(Assumption
F.5
).

If
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
, we have
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}])
, then by Part 1 we know that
\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2
. By definition of
\psi
function (Definition
F.4
), if
\psi(x)<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2
, we have
|x|<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
.
∎

Fact F.7

(Characterization of
k
(Line
7
),
v^{\new}
(Line
7
) of
UpdateV
)
.

In the
j
-th iteration,
\forall i\in[n]
, let
y_{i}=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v_{i}^{(j)}-1)+\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
. Let
\pi:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting such that
y_{\pi(i)}\geq y_{\pi(i+1)}
. We have the following guarantees from the description of procedure
SoftThreshold
(Algorithm
7
).

\displaystyle 1.
