\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\{i\in[n]:\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\right\}\subseteq\pi([k])

\displaystyle 2.

\displaystyle\penalty\ v^{\new}_{i}=\begin{cases}w^{(j+1)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([k]);\\
v^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle 3.

\displaystyle\penalty\ \forall i\notin\pi([k]),\penalty\ |w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{\new}_{i}-1|<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}.

Proof.

Part 1 and 2.

From Line
7
of
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
)
v^{\new}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the output of

\displaystyle\textsc{SoftThreshold}(y_{i}\leftarrow\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),w^{(j+1)},v^{(j)},\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}{32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}},n^{a}).

The statements then follow from a similar proof as that of Part 1 and 2 of Fact
F.6
.

Part 3.
From Part 2,
v^{\new}_{i}
has two possible values. If
v^{\new}_{i}=w^{(j+1)}_{i}
, we have
|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{\new}_{i}-1|=0
.

If
v^{\new}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}
, we have
i\notin\pi([k])
, then by Part 1 we know that
\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)<\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/2
(Assumption
F.5
). Then
|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
by Definition
F.4
.
∎

Fact F.8

(
\widetilde{v}
is far away from
v
)
.

For any
j\in\{0,...,T\}
, for any
i\in[n]
, either
v^{(j)}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
, or

|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|>\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}
.

Proof.

We prove it by induction. When initializing, we set
v^{(0)}=\widetilde{v}^{(0)}
(Line
14
of
Initialize
, Algorithm
5
), so the statement is true when
j=0
.

In later iterations,
v
and
\widetilde{v}
can only be modified by procedure
UpdateV
. And in
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
), the if branch on Line
6
ensures that we can enter at most one of
MatrixUpdate
or
PartialMatrexUpdate
. Now we analyze iteration
j
by looking into these two cases.

Case 1.
When we enter Procedure
MatrixUpdate
, we always set
v\leftarrow v^{\new}
and
\widetilde{v}\leftarrow v^{\new}
(Line
13
of Algorithm
13
). So
v^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}
, and the statement holds in this case.

Case 2.
When we enter Procedure
PartialMatrexUpdate
, we know we did not enter Procedure
MatrixUpdate
due to the else-if branch, and we do not modify
v
in
PartialMatrexUpdate
, so
v
does not change, i.e.,
v^{(j+1)}=v^{(j)}
.

And for
\widetilde{v}
, we set
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}\leftarrow\widetilde{v}^{\new}
(Line
16
in Algorithm
14
), where
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
is defined on Line
5
of Procedure
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
). By Part 3 of Fact
F.6
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}\leftarrow\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\begin{cases}v^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
w^{(j+1)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ but\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\notin[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise}.\end{cases}

In the first case, we have
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}=v^{(j+1)}_{i}
, and the lemma statement holds.

In the second case, we have
|\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j+1)}_{i}-1|=|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|>\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}
, and the lemma statement is also true.

In the third case we have
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
. The lemma statement is true by induction hypothesis.
∎

The following corollary follows from the above fact and triangle inequality:

Corollary F.9
.

For any
j\in\{0,\dots,T\}
and any
i\in[n]
, if
v^{(j)}_{i}\neq\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
, then
\forall x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2.

Proof.

Since
v^{(j)}_{i}\neq\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
, from Fact
F.8
, we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|>\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}.

(47)

We consider the following two cases depend on the value of
|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|
:

Case 1,
|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|\leq 1
:

We have

\displaystyle|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|=|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|\cdot|\frac{v^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|\geq|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|\geq|\frac{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|>\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}.

where the second step follows from the assumption of Case 1 that
|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|\leq 1
, the
third step follows from triangle inequality, and the fourth step follows from Eq. (
47
).

Case 2,
|\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|>1
:
We have

\displaystyle|\frac{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{|{(\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i})}/{v^{(j)}_{i}}|}{|{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}/{v^{(j)}_{i}}|}\geq\penalty\ \frac{|{(\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i})}/{v^{(j)}_{i}}|}{|{(\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i})}/{v^{(j)}_{i}}|+1}\geq\penalty\ \frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}+1}\geq\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2.

where the second step follows from triangle inequality, the third step follows from Eq. (
47
) and the fact that function
\frac{x}{x+1}
is monotonically increasing, and the last step follows from
\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}\leq 1
.

Then similar as Case 1 we have

\displaystyle|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|=

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|\cdot|\frac{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}|\geq|\frac{x-\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|+|\frac{x-v^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|\geq|\frac{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-v^{(j)}_{i}}{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}}|>\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2.

∎

Fact F.10

(Lower bound on
k_{j}
)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we have that either
k_{j}=0
, or
k_{j}\geq n^{a}
.

Proof.

Recall that
k_{j}
is the second returned value by
UpdateV
on Line
4
of
UpdateQuery
(Algorithm
8
). If in
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
) we do not enter the if-branch on Line
6
, then by the return clauses on Line
19
and Line
22
, we have that
k_{j}=0
.

Now we only need to prove that when we enter the if-branch on Line
6
of
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
), the returned value
k_{j}=k\geq n^{a}
. When the if-clause on Line
6
is true, we have

\displaystyle|\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})|\geq n^{a},

(48)

where
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
is defined on Line
5
.
\forall i\in[n]
, let
y_{i}=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v_{i}^{(j)}-1)+\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
. Let
\pi:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting such that
y_{\pi(i)}\geq y_{\pi(i+1)}
. From Part 1 of Fact
F.7
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{i\in[n]:\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\right\}\subseteq\pi([k]).

(49)

Now it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})\subseteq\left\{i:\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\right\},

(50)

because then we would have

\displaystyle k_{j}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ k=|\pi([k])|\geq\left|\left\{i:\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})|\geq n^{a},

where the first step follows from the definition of
k_{j}
, the third step follows from Eq. (
49
), the fourth step follows from Eq. (
50
), and the fifth step follows from Eq. (
48
).

Now it remains to prove Eq. (
50
). We first prove that

\displaystyle\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})\subseteq\left\{i:|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|+|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2\right\}.

Using Part 3 of Fact
F.6
we know that
\widetilde{v}_{i}^{\new}
can be
v^{(j)}_{i}
,
w^{(j+1)}_{i}
, or
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
. So for any
i\in\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})
, since
\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}\neq v^{(j)}_{i}
, we know that
\widetilde{v}_{i}^{\new}
is either
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}
or
w_{i}^{(j+1)}
.
We consider these two cases:

1.

\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}\neq v^{(j)}_{i}
.
Using Corollary
F.9
, and plugging in
x\leftarrow w^{(j+1)}_{i}
we directly have that
|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|+|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2
.

2.

\widetilde{v}_{i}^{\new}=w_{i}^{(j+1)}\neq v^{(j)}_{i}
. By Part 3 of Fact
F.6
we have
w_{i}^{(j+1)}\notin[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}]
,
which then gives us

|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2
.

Thus we always have that
\forall i\in\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-v^{(j)})
,
|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|+|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2
.
So at least one of the following is true:

\displaystyle|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4\mathrm{\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ or\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ }|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4.

Without loss of generality, assume
|w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}}{4}
. We have

\displaystyle\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\penalty\ \psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)\geq\penalty\ \psi(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4)=\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}).

where the first two steps follow from
\psi
is non-negative and non-decreasing, and the third step follows from
\psi(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
(see Definition
F.4
of
\psi
). Thus this proves Eq. (
50
).
∎

Fact F.11

(Lower bound on
\widetilde{k}_{j}
)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we have that either
\widetilde{k}_{j}=0
, or
\widetilde{k}_{j}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\widetilde{k}_{j}
is the third returned value by
UpdateV
on Line
4
of
UpdateQuery
(Algorithm
8
). If in
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
) we do not enter the else-if branch on Line
14
, then by the return clauses on Line
12
and Line
22
, we have that
\widetilde{k}_{j}=0
.

Now we only need to prove that when we enter the else-if branch on Line
14
of
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
), the returned value
\widetilde{k}_{j}=\widetilde{k}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
. When the if-clause on Line
14
is true, we have

|\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v}^{(j)})|\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}.

From Part 3 of Fact
F.6
, we have
|\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v}^{(j)})|\subseteq\pi([\widetilde{k}])
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{k}_{j}=\widetilde{k}\geq|\supp(\widetilde{v}^{\new}-\widetilde{v}^{(j)})|\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}.

∎

Fact F.12

(Characterization of
MatrixUpdate
)
.

Assume Assumption
F.5
is true.
In the
j
-th iteration,
\forall i\in[n]
, let
y_{i}=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v_{i}^{(j)}-1)+\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
.
Let
\pi:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting such that
y_{\pi(i)}\geq y_{\pi(i+1)}
. If
k_{j}>0
, we have the following:

1.

k_{j}
satisfies that either
k_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(k_{j})}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}
.

2.

y_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
.

3.

After the procedure
MatrixUpdate
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}=v^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}=w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)},&\forall i\leq k_{j};\\
\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}=\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)},&\forall i>k_{j}.\end{cases}

4.

The running time of
MatrixUpdate
in the
j
th iteration is
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(k_{j},n^{1+o(1)},n)
.

Proof.

Part 1 and Part 2.

Note that as long as
k_{j}>0
,
k_{j}
is the
k
computed in Line
7
in Procedure
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
). By Fact
F.10
and
k_{j}\neq 0
, we have
k_{j}\geq n^{a}
. Therefore, when calculating
k
using one call of
SoftThreshold
(Line
7
in Algorithm
9
), we must have entered the repeat-until branch (Line
7
to
9
in Algorithm
7
). So
k_{j}
must satisfy the end condition of the repeat-loop that either
k_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(k_{j})}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}
. This finishes the proof of Part 1.

Further, let
k^{*}
denote the largest index such that
y_{\pi(k^{*})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
. We have

\displaystyle y_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1-1/\log n)^{\log_{1.5}k_{j}-\log_{1.5}k^{*}}\cdot y_{\pi(k^{*})}\geq(1-1/\log n)^{\log_{1.5}n}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}{32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}{3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}},

where the first step follows from the repeat-loop (Line
8
in Algorithm
7
), the second step follows from
\log_{1.5}k_{j}-\log_{1.5}k^{*}\leq\log_{1.5}n
and
y_{\pi(k^{*})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
, and the last step follows from the fact that for
n\geq 4
,
(1-1/\log n)^{\log_{1.5}n}\geq 1/100
. This finishes the proof of Part 2.

Part 3.
From Line
13
of
MatrixUpdate
(Algorithm
13
) we have that
v^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}=v^{\new}
.
Using Part 2 of Fact
F.7
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v^{\new}_{\pi(i)}=w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)},&\forall i\leq k_{j};\\
v^{\new}_{\pi(i)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)},&\forall i>k_{j},\end{cases}

(51)

so we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}=v^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}=w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)},&\forall i\leq k_{j};\\
\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)},&\forall i>k_{j}.\end{cases}

Note that by Part 1, either
k_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(k_{j})}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}
. If
k_{j}=n
, there is no
i>k_{j}
, so the proof is already finished.

Otherwise, for any
i>k_{j}
, we prove that
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}=v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
by contradiction. If
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\neq v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
, using Corollary
F.9
and plugging in
x\leftarrow w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}
, we have
|w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1|+|w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/2
,
so at least one of the following is true:

\displaystyle|w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4\mathrm{\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ or\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ }|w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1|\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4.

Thus we have

\displaystyle y_{\pi(i)}=\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)\geq\psi(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}),

where the first two steps follow from
\psi
is non-negative and non-decreasing, and the third step follows from
\psi(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}/4)=\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
(see Definition
F.4
of
\psi
).

Then we have

y_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq y_{\pi(i)}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
since
y
is decreasingly sorted according to
\pi
and
i>k_{j}
. But from the initial assignment of
k_{j}
on Line
5
of
SoftThreshold
(Algorithm
7
), and that
k_{j}
can only strictly increase afterwards, we have that
y_{\pi(k_{j})}<\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
. This leads to contradiction. Thus we have
\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}=\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)},\penalty\ \forall i>k_{j}
.

Part 4.

This directly follows from Lemma
E.12
which proves the running time of
MatrixUpdate
per call.
∎

Fact F.13

(Characterization of
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

Assume Assumption
F.5
is true. In the
j
-th iteration,
\forall i\in[n]
, let
y_{i}=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
. Let
\pi:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting such that
y_{\pi(i)}\geq y_{\pi(i+1)}
. If
\widetilde{k}_{j}>0
,
we have the following:

1.

\widetilde{k}_{j}
satisfies that either
\widetilde{k}_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5)}
.

2.

y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/100
.

3.

After the procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
,
\forall i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(200\log n),

(52)

and
\forall i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}
. Also,
\forall i\in[n]
,
v^{(j+1)}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}
.

4.

The running time of
PartialMatrixUpdate
in the
j
-th iteration is
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k}_{j},n^{a},n^{a})
.

Proof.

Part 1 and 2.
Note that as long as
\widetilde{k}_{j}>0
,
\widetilde{k}_{j}
is the
\widetilde{k}
computed in Line
4
in Procedure
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
). By Fact
F.11
, we have
\widetilde{k}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
. So by a similar argument as that of the proof Part 1 and 2 of Fact
F.12
, we can prove Part 1 and 2 of this fact.

Part 3.

Because we do not modify
v
in
PartialMatrixUpdate
, so
\forall i\in[n]
,
v^{(j+1)}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}
.

In procedure
PartialMatrixUpdate
, we set
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}\leftarrow\widetilde{v}^{\new}
(Line
16
of Algorithm
14
), where
\widetilde{v}^{\new}
is created by one call to
SoftThreshold
(Line
4
in
UpdateV
, Algorithm
8
). By Part 3 of Fact
F.6
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}\leftarrow\begin{cases}v^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
w^{(j+1)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}])\mathrm{\penalty\ but\penalty\ }w^{(j+1)}_{i}\notin[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}];\\
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i},&\penalty\ \mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise}.\end{cases}

For
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}
.

For
i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
, if
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=w^{(j+1)}_{i}
, Eq. (
52
) is trivially true since
\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)=0
.

Otherwise
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}=\widetilde{v}^{\new}_{i}=v^{(j)}_{i}
and
w^{(j+1)}_{i}\in[(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i},(1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v^{(j)}_{i}]
, we have:

\displaystyle\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)\leq\psi(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})=\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\leq(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(200\log n)),

where the last step is by
\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(100\log n)
(Assumption
F.5
).

Part 4.

This directly follows from Lemma
E.18
which proves the running time of
PartialMatrixUpdate
per call.
∎

Corollary F.14
.

Assume Assumption
F.5
is true. In the
j
-th iteration, if we enter
PartialMatrixUpdate
, we must have
\forall i\in[n]
,
\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}-1)\leq\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
.

Proof.

If we enter
PartialMatrixUpdate
, we must have
\widetilde{k}_{j}>0
. By Part 2,3 of Fact
F.13
, there is some permutation
\pi
such that
\forall i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,

\displaystyle\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/100\text{\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ and\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ }\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(200\log n)<\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/100.

And also by Part 3 of Fact
F.13
,
\forall i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}
. Therefore,
\forall i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
, we have that
\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)=\psi(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/\widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}-1)
.
∎

F.3
Amortized analysis for
MatrixUpdate

F.3.1
Definitions

Definition F.15

(
x
and
y
for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

For any
j\in\{0,1,\dots,T-1\}
, and for any
i\in[n]
, we define
x^{(j)}_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{i}
as follows:

\displaystyle x^{(j)}_{i}:=\psi(w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ y^{(j)}_{i}:=\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),

where
v^{(j)}
,
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
and
w^{(j)}
are defined as of Definition
F.1
.

Note that the difference between
x^{(j)}_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{i}
is that
w
changes from
w^{(j)}
to
w^{(j+1)}
. The difference between
y^{(j)}_{i}
and
x^{(j+1)}_{i}
is that
v
and
\widetilde{v}
changes from
v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
to
v^{(j+1)},\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}
.

For convenience, we define permutations of the coordinates that are sorted according to
x
or
y
.

Definition F.16

(Sorting permutations for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

For any
j\in\{0,1,\dots,T\}
, let
\tau_{j}
be the permutation such that
x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i)}\geq x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i+1)}
, and let
\pi_{j}
be the permutation such that
y^{(j)}_{\pi_{j}(i)}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi_{j}(i+1)}
.

When it is clear from the context that we are only arguing about the
j
-th iteration, for simplicity we assume the coordinates of vector
x^{(j)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are sorted such that
x^{(j)}_{i}\geq x^{(j)}_{i+1}
. And we use
\tau
and
\pi
to denote the permutations such that
x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\geq x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i+1)}
and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+1)}
.

Definition F.17

(
g
for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

For some
a\leq\alpha
, where
\alpha
is the dual exponent of matrix multiplication, we define
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
as follows:

\displaystyle g_{i}=\begin{cases}n^{-a},&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\leq n^{a};\\
i^{\frac{\omega-2}{1-a}-1}\cdot n^{-\frac{a(\omega-2)}{1-a}},&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in(n^{a},n].\end{cases}

Note that
g
is non-increasing. For all
k_{j}\in(n^{a},n]
,
(k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}n^{2})=k_{j}^{\frac{\omega-2}{1-a}}\cdot n^{2-\frac{a(\omega-2)}{1-a}}
is an upper bound of the running time
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,k_{j})
of multiplying a
n\times n
matrix with a
n\times k_{j}
matrix. For more details please refer to
[
GU18
]
.

Definition F.18

(Potential function
\Phi
for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

We define the potential function in the
j
-th iteration as

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i)}.

Note that we always have
\Phi_{j}\geq 0
since
\forall i
,
g_{i}
and
x_{i}^{(j)}
are both non-negative.

F.3.2
Main result

Lemma F.19

(Amortized time for
MatrixUpdate
)
.

Let sequences
\{w^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{v^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be defined as of Definition
F.1
, let
k_{j}
be defined as of Definition
F.3
,
and let
\{x^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{y^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
g
,
\Phi
be defined as of Definition
F.15
,
F.17
and
F.18
.
If we further have the condition that the input sequence satisfies the following:
\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[w_{i}^{(j+1)}|w^{(j)}]/w_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\leq C_{1}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ w^{(j)}])^{2}\leq C_{2}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1|\leq 1/4.

Then, we have that in expectation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}k_{j}g_{k_{j}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\Big((\frac{C_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}})\cdot\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big).

Further, combining with Lemma
E.12
, the expected amortized running time per iteration of
MatrixUpdate
is

O\Big((\frac{C_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}})\cdot(n^{2-a/2}+n^{\omega-1/2})\log n\Big).

Proof.

First note that in the
j
-th iteration, the value of the potential
\Phi_{j}
depends on
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
. And the value of
v^{(j)}
and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
are affected by both
MatrixUpdate
and
PartialMatrixUpdate
. We upper bound how much the potential function can increase due to changing
w^{(j)}
to
w^{(j+1)}
in Section
F.3.3
, and we also lower bound how much the potential function can decrease because of changing
v^{(j)}
to
v^{(j+1)}
and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
to
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}
in Section
F.3.4
.

In the beginning
v^{(0)}=\widetilde{v}^{(0)}=w^{(0)}
, so
\Phi_{0}=0
. Also note that
\Phi_{j}\geq 0,\forall j\in[T]
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\E[\Phi_{T}]-\Phi_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\E[\Phi_{j+1}-\Phi_{j}]=\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\underbrace{\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\right]}_{w\text{\penalty\ move}}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\underbrace{\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\right]}_{v,\widetilde{v}\text{\penalty\ move}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\Big(O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}-\Omega\left(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}/(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n)\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ T\cdot O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\|g\|_{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{T}\Omega\left({\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}}/(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n)\right),

where the second step follows from splitting terms and the fact that
\Phi_{0}
is deterministic, the third step follows from the definition of
\Phi
(Definition
F.18
), the fourth step follows from splitting terms, the fifth step follows from Lemma
F.20
which states that
\forall w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2},

then this upper bound also holds for unconditional expectation, the fifth step also follows from Lemma
F.24
which states that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq\Omega({\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}}/(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n))
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}k_{j}g_{k_{j}}\leq O\Big((\frac{C_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}})\cdot\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big).

Using Lemma
E.12
, we have that the expected amortized running time per iteration of
MatrixUpdate
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,k_{j})\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{n^{2}}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}k_{j}g_{k_{j}}=O\Big((\frac{C_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}})\cdot n^{2}\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\Big((\frac{C_{1}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}})\cdot(n^{2-a/2}+n^{\omega-1/2})\log n\Big),

where the first step follows from the definition of
g
which gives that
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,k_{j})\leq n^{2}k_{j}g_{k_{j}}
, and the third step follows from Lemma
F.25
that
\|g\|_{2}=O(n^{-a/2}+n^{\omega-5/2})
.
∎

F.3.3
w
move

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
F.20
.

Lemma F.20

(
w
move)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, for any possible values
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\|g\|_{2}.

(53)

Proof.

For simplicity, in this proof we write
\E[\cdot]
as a shorthand of
\E[\cdot|w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}]
.

Observe that since the non-negative values
x^{(j)}_{i}
are sorted in descending order, and
g
is also non-increasing, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}x^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}x^{(j)}_{i}.

(54)

We then have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)+\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)]-\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)+\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)]+\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)]

where the first step follows from Eq.(
54
), the second step follows from the definitions of
x^{(j)}
and
y^{(j)}
(Definition
F.15
).

Now
\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}-x_{i}^{(j)}]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\|g\|_{2}
directly follows from Lemma
F.21
and Lemma
F.22
.
∎

It remains to prove the following two lemmas.

Lemma F.21
.

Under Assumption
F.5
, in the
j
-th iteration, for any
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)\penalty\ |\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}]=O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

Proof.

For simplicity, in this proof we write
\E[\cdot]
as a shorthand of
\E[\cdot|w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}]
. And we also define
x
and
y
as

\displaystyle y_{i}=w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ x_{i}=w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1,

(55)

and they are only used in this proof. Then the lemma statement becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{\pi(i)})-\psi(x_{\pi(i)})]=O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

Let
I
be the set of indices such that
|x_{i}|\leq 1
. We separate the term into two:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{\pi(i)})-\psi(x_{\pi(i)})]=\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})]+\sum_{i\in I^{c}}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})].

Case 1: Terms from
I
.

Mean value theorem shows that for any
y_{i}
, there exist
\zeta
such that

\displaystyle\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \psi^{\prime}(x_{i})(y_{i}-x_{i})+\frac{1}{2}\psi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)(y_{i}-x_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{w^{(j+1)}_{i}-w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\cdot(\frac{w^{(j+1)}_{i}-w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}})^{2},

where the second step follows from plugging in the definition of
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
in Eq. (
55
), and letting
L_{2}=\max_{x}\psi^{\prime\prime}(x)
.
Taking conditional expectation (over
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
) on both sides, we get

\displaystyle\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})]\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{\E[w^{(j+1)}_{i}]-w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\frac{1}{(v^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}\E[(w^{(j+1)}_{i}-w^{(j)}_{i})^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}\cdot\beta_{i}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\frac{(w^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}{(v^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}\gamma_{i},

where
\beta_{i}
and
\gamma_{i}
are defined as
\beta_{i}=\E[w^{(j+1)}_{i}]/w^{(j)}_{i}-1
,
\gamma_{i}=\E[(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/w^{(j)}_{i}-1)^{2}]
.

This then gives us

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})]\leq\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}\cdot\beta_{i}+\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\frac{L_{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{(w^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}{(v^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}\cdot\gamma_{i}.

(56)

For the term
w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}
, we note that for
i\in I
, we have

\displaystyle|w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|=\penalty\ |x_{i}+1|\leq\penalty\ |x_{i}|+1\leq\penalty\ 2,

(57)

where the first step follows the definition of
x_{i}
in Eq. (
55
), the second step follows from triangle inequality, and the third step follows from
|x_{i}|\leq 1
for
i\in I
.

Using this, we can bound the first term of Eq. (
56
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}\cdot\beta_{i}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Big(\sum_{i\in I}\big(g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\psi^{\prime}(x_{i})\cdot\frac{w^{(j)}_{i}}{v^{(j)}_{i}}\big)^{2}\cdot\sum_{i\in I}\beta_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Big(\sum_{i\in I}(2L_{1}\cdot g_{\pi^{-1}(i)})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i\in I}\beta_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(L_{1})\cdot\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{2}\cdot C_{1}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}=\penalty\ O(C_{1}L_{1}\|g\|_{2}),

(58)

where
L_{1}=\max_{x}|\psi^{\prime}(x)|
, the first step follows by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the second step follows by
|\psi^{\prime}(x_{i})|\leq L_{1}
and
|w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|\leq 2
by Eq.(
57
), and the third step follows from
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta^{2}_{i}\leq C_{1}^{2}
from the lemma statement of Lemma
F.19
.

For the second term of Eq. (
56
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\frac{L_{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{(w^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}{(v^{(j)}_{i})^{2}}\cdot\gamma_{i}\leq O(L_{2})\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\gamma_{i}\leq O(L_{2})\cdot\|g\|_{2}\cdot\|\gamma\|_{2}=O(C_{2}L_{2}\|g\|_{2}),

(59)

where the first step follows from
|w^{(j)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|\leq 2
by Eq.(
57
), the second step follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the third step follows from
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}^{2}\leq C_{2}^{2}
from the lemma statement of Lemma
F.19
.

Now, plugging the bound of Eq. (
F.3.3
) and Eq. (
59
) into Eq. (
56
) and using that
L_{1}=O(1)
,
L_{2}=O(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})
(from Part 4 of Lemma
F.37
), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

Case 2: Terms from
I^{c}
.

For all
i\in I^{c}
, we have
|x_{i}|>1
.
Note that
\psi(x)
is a constant for
x\geq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
, and from Assumption
F.5
we assume that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\leq 1/4
.
Therefore, if
|y_{i}|\geq 1/2
, we have that
\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})=0
.
Hence, we only need to consider the
i\in I^{c}
such that
|y_{i}|<1/2
. For these
i
, we have that

\displaystyle|y_{i}-x_{i}|\geq|x_{i}|-|y_{i}|>1/2,

(60)

where the first step follows by triangle inequality, and the second step follows from the assumptions
|x_{i}|>1
and
|y_{i}|<1/2
. We also have

\displaystyle|y_{i}-x_{i}|=\left|(w^{(j+1)}_{i}-w^{(j)}_{i})/v^{(j)}_{i}\right|=\left|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}\right|\cdot\left|(w^{(j+1)}_{i}-w^{(j)}_{i})/w^{(j+1)}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}\left|1-w^{(j)}_{i}/w^{(j+1)}_{i}\right|,

(61)

where the first step follows from the definition of
y_{i}
and
x_{i}
in Eq. (
55
), the third step follows from
|y_{i}|=|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}-1|<1/2
and thus
|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/v^{(j)}_{i}|<3/2
.

Combining Eq. (
60
) and Eq. (
61
), we have that
|1-w^{(j)}_{i}/w^{(j+1)}_{i}|>1/3
and hence
|w^{(j)}_{i}/w^{(j+1)}_{i}|<2/3
or
|w^{(j)}_{i}/w^{(j+1)}_{i}|>4/3
, which then gives us
|w^{(j+1)}_{i}/w^{(j)}_{i}-1|>1/4
, but this contradicts with the lemma statement of Lemma
F.19
, so
|y_{i}|<1/2
is impossible.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I^{c}}g_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\cdot\E[\psi(y_{i})-\psi(x_{i})]=0.

Combining both cases, we have the result.
∎

Lemma F.22
.

In the
j
-th iteration, for any
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]=O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is exactly the same as that of Lemma
F.21
, just replace all
v
with
\widetilde{v}
in the proof of Lemma
F.21
.
∎

Note that in the proof of Lemma
F.21
we do not have any requirement on
v
, so in fact we have the following more generalized lemma.

Lemma F.23

(Generalized “
w
move” lemma)
.

Let
\{w^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be a random sequence that satisfies

\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\E[w_{i}^{(j+1)}|w^{(j)}]/w_{i}^{(j)}-1\Big)^{2}\leq C_{1}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\E[(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ w^{(j)}]\Big)^{2}\leq C_{2}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ |w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1|\leq 1/4.

Let
\{v^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be another random sequence such that
\forall j\in[T]
,
v^{(j)}
only depends on
w^{(j)}
and
v^{(j-1)}
. Let
\psi
be defined as of Definition
F.4
, where the parameter
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}<1/4
. Let
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a sequence that is non-increasing, i.e.,
g_{1}\geq g_{2}\geq\cdots\geq g_{n}
. Let
\pi_{j}:[n]\to[n]
be the sorting
\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)\geq\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i+1)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi(i+1)}-1)
.

Then
\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}
, in the
j
-th iteration, for any
w^{(j)}
and
v^{(j)}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi_{j}(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi_{j}(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi_{j}(i)}/v^{(j)}_{\pi_{j}(i)}-1)\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)}\right]=O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

F.3.4
v,\widetilde{v}
move

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
F.24
.

Lemma F.24

(
v,\widetilde{v}
move)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n}\Big),

in which
\pi,\tau:[n]\to[n]
are permutations such that
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+1)}
and
x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\geq x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i+1)}
.

Proof.

Case 1,
k_{j}=0
.
When
k_{j}=0
, we didn’t enter the
MatrixUpdate
procedure, so
v^{(j+1)}=v^{(j)}
. If we further enter the
PartialMatrixUpdate
procedure and change
\widetilde{v}
, we have that
\forall i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{i}-x^{(j+1)}_{i}=\penalty\ \psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)-\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)\geq\penalty\ 0,

where the first step follows by the definition of
x^{(j+1)}_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{i}
(Definition
F.15
) and the fact that
v
do not change, and the last step follows by Corollary
F.14
.

Thus
y^{(j)}_{i}\geq x^{(j+1)}_{i}
,
\forall i\in[n]
. Since
g_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
are both non-increasing, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\tau(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq 0=\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}/(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n)),

where the last step follows by
k_{j}=0
.

Case 2,
k_{j}\neq 0
.

When
k_{j}\neq 0
, we must have entered
MatrixUpdate
, and by Fact
F.10
, we must have
k_{j}\geq n^{a}
.
By Part 3 of Fact
F.12
, the difference between
x^{(j+1)}
and
y^{(j)}
is that in coordinates
i\in\pi([k_{j}])
,
x^{(j+1)}_{i}
is cleared to
0
, and in other coordinates
x^{(j+1)}_{i}
is the same with
y^{(j)}_{i}
. So we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+k_{j})}).

(62)

Note that when the subscripts are out of range, we define
y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+1)}=\dots=y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+k_{j})}=0
.

Part 2 of Fact
F.12
shows that

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}).

(63)

Part 1 of Fact
F.12
shows that either
k_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(k_{j})}^{(j)}<(1-1/\log n)y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}^{(j)}
. If
k_{j}=n
, we let
L=k_{j}=n
, otherwise we let
L=k_{j}/1.5
.
The
L
we choose always satisfied that
for all
i\in[L]
,

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+k_{j})}\geq\penalty\ y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(1+k_{j})}\geq\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n),

(64)

where the first step follows by
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
is non-increasing, the second step is true because:

1.

In the case of
k_{j}=n
, we have
y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}=y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}{3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}
by Eq. (
63
) and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j}+1)}=y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+1)}=0
.

2.

In the case of
y_{\pi(k_{j})}^{(j)}<(1-1/\log n)y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(1+k_{j})}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j})}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}/\log n\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}/(3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n),

where the second step follows from the inequality of
y^{(j)}_{\pi(k_{j})}
, and the third step follows from Eq. (
63
) and the fact that
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
is non-increasing.

Putting it all together, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+k_{j})})\geq\sum_{i=1}^{L}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+k_{j})})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{L}g_{i}\cdot\Big(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}}{3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n}\Big)=\Omega\Big(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2}\cdot k_{j}\cdot g_{k_{j}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\log n}\Big),

where the first step is by Eq. (
62
), the second step follows from
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
is non-increasing and thus all terms
\geq 0
, the third step is by Eq. (
64
), and the last step follows from
g_{L}\geq g_{k_{j}}
and
L=\Omega(k_{j})
.
∎

F.3.5
\ell_{2}
-norm of
g

Lemma F.25

(
\ell_{2}
-norm of
g
)
.

g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
(Definition
F.17
) satisfies
\|g\|_{2}=O(n^{-a/2}+n^{\omega-5/2})
.

Proof.

For
i\leq n^{a}
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{n^{a}}g_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n^{a}}n^{-2a}=n^{-a}
.

For
i>n^{a}
, note that there exists
i\in[n]
such that
i>n^{a}
implies
a<1
, so we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=n^{a}+1}^{n}g_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=n^{a}+1}^{n}i^{\frac{2(\omega-2)}{1-a}-2}\cdot n^{-\frac{2a(\omega-2)}{1-a}}=O(1)\cdot\int_{n^{a+1}}^{n}x^{\frac{2(\omega-2)}{1-a}-2}\cdot n^{-\frac{2a(\omega-2)}{1-a}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(1)\cdot\max\{n^{\frac{2(\omega-2)}{1-a}-1},n^{\frac{2a(\omega-2)}{1-a}-a}\}\cdot n^{-\frac{2a(\omega-2)}{1-a}}=O(n^{2\omega-5}+n^{-a}).

Therefore, we have
\|g\|_{2}=O(n^{-a/2}+n^{\omega-5/2})
.
∎

F.4
Amortized analysis for
PartialMatrixUpdate

F.4.1
Definitions

Definition F.26

(
x
and
y
for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

For any
j\in\{0,1,\dots,T-1\}
, and for any
i\in[n]
, we define
x^{(j)}_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{i}
as follows:

\displaystyle x^{(j)}_{i}:=\psi(w^{(j)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ y^{(j)}_{i}:=\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1),

where
v^{(j)}
,
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
and
w^{(j)}
are defined as of Definition
F.1
.

Note that the difference between
x^{(j)}_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{i}
is that
w
is changing. The difference between
y^{(j)}_{i}
and
x^{(j+1)}_{i}
is that
\widetilde{v}
is changing.

Definition F.27

(Sorting permutations for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

For any
j\in\{0,1,\dots,T\}
, let
\tau_{j}
be the permutation that
x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i)}\geq x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i+1)}
, and let
\pi_{j}
be the permutation that
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+1)}
.

When it is clear from the context that we are only arguing about the
j
-th iteration, for simplicity we assume the coordinates of vector
x^{(j)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are sorted such that
x^{(j)}_{i}\geq x^{(j)}_{i+1}
. And we use
\tau
and
\pi
to denote the permutations such that
x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\geq x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i+1)}
and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+1)}
.

Definition F.28

(
g
for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

For some
\widetilde{a}\leq\alpha\cdot a
, where
\alpha
is the dual exponent of matrix multiplication, and
a
is the parameter for
MatrixUpdate
, we define
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
as follows:

\displaystyle g_{i}=\begin{cases}n^{-\widetilde{a}},&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\leq n^{\widetilde{a}};\\
i^{\frac{a(\omega-2)}{a-\widetilde{a}}-1}\cdot n^{-\frac{a\widetilde{a}(\omega-2)}{a-\widetilde{a}}},&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i\in(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a}];\\
0&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }i>n^{a}.\end{cases}

Note that
g
is non-increasing. For all
\widetilde{k}_{j}\in(n^{\widetilde{a}},n^{a}]
,
(\widetilde{k}_{j}\cdot g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}n^{2a})=\widetilde{k}_{j}^{\frac{a(\omega-2)}{a-\widetilde{a}}}\cdot n^{2a-\frac{a\widetilde{a}(\omega-2)}{a-\widetilde{a}}}
is an upper bound of
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},\widetilde{k}_{j})
of multiplying a
n^{a}\times n^{a}
matrix with a
n^{a}\times\widetilde{k}_{j}
matrix. For more details please refer to
[
GU18
]
.

Definition F.29

(Potential function
\Phi
for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

We define the potential function in the
j
-th iteration as

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot x^{(j)}_{\tau_{j}(i)}.

Note that we always have
\Phi_{j}\geq 0
since
\forall i
,
g_{i}
and
x_{i}^{(j)}
are both non-negative.

F.4.2
Main result

Lemma F.30

(Amortized time for
PartialMatrixUpdate
)
.

Let sequences
\{w^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{v^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be defined as of Definition
F.1
, let
\widetilde{k}_{j}
be defined as of Definition
F.3
,
and let
\{x^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{y^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
g
,
\Phi
be defined as of Definition
F.15
,
F.28
and
F.29
.
If we further have the condition that the input sequence satisfies the following:
\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[w_{i}^{(j+1)}|w^{(j)}]/w_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\leq C_{1}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[(w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ w^{(j)}])^{2}\leq C_{2}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |w_{i}^{(j+1)}/w_{i}^{(j)}-1|\leq 1/4.

Then, we have that in expectation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}=O\Big((C_{1}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big).

Further, combining with Lemma
E.18
, the expected amortized running time per iteration of
PartialMatrixUpdate
is

O\Big((C_{1}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot(n^{1+a-\widetilde{a}/2}+n^{1+(\omega-3/2)a})\log n\Big).

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
F.19
, we upper bound how much the potential function can increase due to changing
w^{(j)}
to
w^{(j+1)}
(in Section
F.4.3
), and also lower bound how much the potential function can decrease because of changing
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
to
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}
(in Section
F.4.4
).

Similar to Lemma
F.19
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\E[\Phi_{T}]-\Phi_{0}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\underbrace{\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\right]}_{w\text{\penalty\ move}}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\underbrace{\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\right]}_{\widetilde{v}\text{\penalty\ move}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\Big(O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}-\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}/\log n)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ T\cdot O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\|g\|_{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{T}\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}/\log n),

where the third step follows from Lemma
F.31
which states that
\forall w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2},

then this upper bound also holds for unconditional expectation, the third step also follows from Lemma
F.33
which states that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\left(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)}\right)\geq\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}/\log n)
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}=O\Big((C_{1}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big).

Using Lemma
E.18
, we have that the expected amortized running time per iteration of
PartialMatrixUpdate
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^{a},\widetilde{k}_{j})\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}n^{1-a}\cdot{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},\widetilde{k}_{j})\leq\frac{n^{1+a}}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\Big((C_{1}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot n^{1+a}\log n\cdot\|g\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\Big((C_{1}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot(n^{1+a-\widetilde{a}/2}+n^{1+(\omega-3/2)a})\log n\Big),

where the first step follows from the fact that we can always divide a
n\times n^{a}
matrix into
n^{1-a}
copies of
n^{a}\times n^{a}
matrices, the second step follows from Definition
F.28
of
g
which gives that
{\cal T}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},n^{a},\widetilde{k}_{j})\leq n^{2a}\cdot\widetilde{k}_{j}g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}
for
n^{\widetilde{a}}\leq\widetilde{k}_{j}\leq n^{a}
, and we indeed have
\widetilde{k}_{j}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
(Fact
F.11
) and
\widetilde{k}_{j}\leq 2n^{a}
(Lemma
E.1
) when entering
PartialMatrixUpdate
. (We ignore the
2
factor since it can only increase the final amortized time by a constant factor.)
The fourth step follows from Lemma
F.34
that
\|g\|_{2}=O(n^{-\widetilde{a}/2}+n^{a\omega-5a/2})
.

∎

F.4.3
w
move

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
F.31
.

Lemma F.31

(
w
move)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, for any possible values
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

(65)

Proof.

For simplicity, in this proof we write
\E[\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ ]
as a shorthand of
\E[\penalty\ \cdot\penalty\ |\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\penalty\ ]
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
F.20
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{i}]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)]

where the first step follows from that the non-negative values
x^{(j)}_{i}
are sorted in descending order, and
g
is also non-increasing, the second step follows from the definitions of
x^{(j)}
and
y^{(j)}
(Definition
F.26
.

Now
\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E[y_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}-x_{i}^{(j)}]\leq O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\|g\|_{2}
directly follows from Lemma
F.32
.
∎

It remains to prove the following Lemma.

Lemma F.32
.

In the
j
-th iteration, for any
w^{(j)}
,
v^{(j)}
, and
\widetilde{v}^{(j)}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot\E\left[\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)-\psi(w^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-1)\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\right]=O(C_{1}+C_{2}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\cdot\|g\|_{2}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is exactly the same as that of Lemma
F.21
, just replace all
v
with
\widetilde{v}
in the proof of Lemma
F.21
.
∎

F.4.4
\widetilde{v}
move

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
F.33
.

Lemma F.33

(
\widetilde{v}
move)
.

In the
j
-th iteration, we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\widetilde{k_{j}}g_{\widetilde{k_{j}}}/\log n).

Proof.

Case 1.
If we enter the
MatrixUpdate
procedure, we have
\widetilde{k}_{j}=0
since we won’t enter the else branch in Line
13
of
UpdateV
(Algorithm
9
). From Part 3 of Fact
F.12
, we know that
\forall i\leq k_{j}
,
\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}=w^{(j+1)}_{\pi(i)}
, and
\forall i>k_{j}
,
\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}=\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}
. Therefore,
\forall i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle x^{(j+1)}_{i}=\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}_{i}-1)\leq\psi(w^{(j+1)}_{i}/\widetilde{v}^{(j)}_{i}-1)=y^{(j)}_{i},

where the first and the third steps follow by the definition of
x^{(j+1)}
and
y^{(j)}
(Definition
F.26
). This means
y^{(j)}_{i}\geq x^{(j+1)}_{i}
,
\forall i\in[n]
. Since
g_{i}
and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
are both non-increasing, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\tau(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq 0=\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\widetilde{k_{j}}g_{\widetilde{k_{j}}}/\log n),

where the last step follows by
\widetilde{k}_{j}=0
.

Case 2.
If we do not enter both the
MatrixUpdate
and the
PartialMatrixUpdate
procedure, nothing happens and
x^{(j+1)}
is the same as
y^{(j)}
, this statement also holds.

Case 3.
Now we only need to consider the case where we enter the
PartialMatrixUpdate
procedure. By Part 3 of Fact
F.13
,
x^{(j+1)}
satisfies that in coordinates
i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
x^{(j+1)}_{i}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}
, and in coordinates
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
,
x^{(j+1)}
is the same with
y^{(j)}
. So we decompose
x^{(j+1)}
into two pieces
x^{(j+1)}=x_{1}+x_{2}
, where
x_{1}
copies the values on coordinates
i\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
and has 0 on other coordinates, and
x_{2}
copies the values on coordinates
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
and has 0 on other coordinates. And when the subscripts are out of range, we define
y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+1)}=\dots=y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+k_{j})}=0
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x_{1,\tau(i)}-x_{2,\tau(i)})\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x_{2,\tau(i)})-\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{k}_{j}}g_{i}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})})-\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{k}_{j}}g_{i}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})})-1.5\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5}g_{i}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n},

(66)

where the second step follows by
x_{1\tau(i)}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}
for
\tau(i)\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
and
x_{1\tau(i)}=0
for
\tau(i)\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
, the third step follows by
x_{2,\tau(i)}=0
for
\tau(i)\in\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
and
x_{2,\tau(i)}=y^{(j)}_{\tau(i)}
for
i\notin\pi([\widetilde{k}_{j}])
, and the last step follows by
g
is non-increasing.

Part 2 of Fact
F.13
shows that

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/100

(67)

Part 1 of Fact
F.13
shows that either
\widetilde{k}_{j}=n
or
y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}^{(j)}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5)}^{(j)}
. If
\widetilde{k}_{j}=n
, we let
L=\widetilde{k}_{j}=n
, otherwise we let
L=\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5
. The
L
we choose always satisfies that
for all
i\in[L]
,

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq\penalty\ y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(1+\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(100\log n),

(68)

where the first step follows by
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
is non-increasing, the second step is true because:

1.

In the case of
\widetilde{k}_{j}=n
, we have
y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}=y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/100
by Eq. (
67
) and
y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j}+1)}=y^{(j)}_{\pi(n+1)}=0
.

2.

In the case of
y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}^{(j)}<(1-1/\log n)\cdot y_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5)}^{(j)}
, we have

\displaystyle y^{(j)}_{\pi(L)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(1+\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}\geq y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}/\log n\geq\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/(100\log n),

where the second step follows from the inequality of
y^{(j)}_{\pi(\widetilde{k}_{j})}
, and the third step follows from Eq. (
67
).

Putting it all together, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-x^{(j+1)}_{\tau(i)})\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})})-1.5\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5}g_{i}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{L}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})})-1.5\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5}g_{i}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{L}g_{i}\cdot(y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}-y^{(j)}_{\pi(i+\widetilde{k}_{j})}-1.5\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{L}g_{i}\cdot(\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{100\log n}-1.5\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{200\log n})=\Omega(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\cdot\widetilde{k}_{j}\cdot g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}/\log n).

where first step is by Eq. (
F.4.4
), the second step follows from
y^{(j)}_{\pi(i)}
is non-increasing and thus all terms
\geq 0
, the third step follows from
L\geq\widetilde{k}_{j}/1.5
, the forth step is by Eq. (
68
), and the last step follows from
g_{L}\geq g_{\widetilde{k}_{j}}
and
L=\Omega(\widetilde{k}_{j})
.
∎

F.4.5
\ell_{2}
-norm of
g

Lemma F.34
.

g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
(Definition
F.28
) satisfies
\|g\|_{2}=O(n^{-\widetilde{a}/2}+n^{a\omega-5a/2})
.

Proof.

The proof is same as that of Lemma
F.25
. We use
n^{a}
to replace
n
, and
n^{\widetilde{a}}
to replace
n^{a}
.
∎

F.5
Amortized analysis for
VectorUpdate

Lemma F.35

(Amortized time for
VectorUpdate
)
.

Let sequences
\{h^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{g^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{\widetilde{g}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be defined as of Definition
F.2
, and let
p_{j}
be defined as of Definition
F.3
.
If we further have the condition that the input sequence satisfies the following:
\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[h_{i}^{(j+1)}|h^{(j)}]/h_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\leq C_{4}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[(h_{i}^{(j+1)}/h_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ h^{(j)}])^{2}\leq C_{5}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |h_{i}^{(j+1)}/h_{i}^{(j)}-1|\leq 1/4.

Then, we have that in expectation

1.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}p_{j}n^{1+o(1)}=O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5+o(1)}\big)
,

2.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0}=O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5a}\big)
.

Further, combining with Lemma
E.27
, the expected amortized running time per iteration of
VectorUpdate
is

O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5+o(1)}\big).

Proof.

Part 1.
For the first equation, we define
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
to be
g_{i}=1
,
\forall i\in[n]
. Note that
g
is non-increasing,
n^{1+o(1)}\cdot(p_{j}g_{p_{j}})=p_{j}n^{1+o(1)}
, and
\|g\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}
. Then we can use the same argument as Lemma
F.19
for
MatrixUpdate
to prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}p_{j}n^{1+o(1)}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1+o(1)}\cdot\|g\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5+o(1)}\big).

Part 2.
For the second equation, we define
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
to be

\displaystyle g_{i}=\begin{cases}n^{-a},&\penalty\ i\leq n^{a},\\
i^{-1},&\penalty\ n^{a}<i<n.\end{cases}

Note that
g
is non-increasing, and
n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0}=n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}\geq n^{a}}\leq n^{2a}\cdot(p_{j}g_{p_{j}})
since analogous to Fact
F.10
we have that either
p_{j}=0
or
p_{j}\geq n^{a}
. We also have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{n^{a}}{n^{2a}}+\int_{n^{a}}^{n}x^{-2}\mathrm{d}x=n^{-a}+n^{-a}-n^{-1}=O(n^{-a}).

Then we can use the same argument as Lemma
F.19
for
MatrixUpdate
to prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0}=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\log n\cdot n^{2a}\|g\|_{2}\big)=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5a}\big).

Combine Part 1 and Part 2.
Using Lemma
E.27
and note that
n^{1.5a}\leq n^{1.5+o(1)}
, we have that the expected amortized running time per iteration of
VectorUpdate
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}(p_{j}n^{1+o(1)}+n^{2a}\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5+o(1)}\big).

∎

F.6
Amortized analysis for
PartialVectorUpdate

Lemma F.36

(Amortized time for
PartialVectorUpdate
)
.

Let sequences
\{h^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{g^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
,
\{\widetilde{g}^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^{T}
be defined as of Definition
F.2
, and let
\widetilde{p}_{j}
be defined as of Definition
F.3
.
If we further have the condition that the input sequence satisfies the following:
\forall j\in\{0,...,T-1\}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[h_{i}^{(j+1)}|h^{(j)}]/h_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\leq C_{4}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{n}(\E[(h_{i}^{(j+1)}/h_{i}^{(j)}-1)^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ h^{(j)}])^{2}\leq C_{5}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |h_{i}^{(j+1)}/h_{i}^{(j)}-1|\leq 1/4.

Then, we have that in expectation

1.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\widetilde{p}_{j}n^{1+o(1)}=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5+o(1)}\big)
,

2.

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{p}_{j}>0}=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{2a-\widetilde{a}/2}\big)
.

Further, combining with Lemma
E.33
, the expected amortized running time per iteration of
PartialVectorUpdate
is

O\big((C_{4}\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{\mathrm{far}}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot(n^{1.5+o(1)}+n^{2a-\widetilde{a}/2})\big).

Proof.

First note that we always have
\widetilde{p}_{j}\leq 2n^{a}
by Lemma
E.1
.

Part 1.
For the first equation, we define
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
to be
g_{i}=1
,
\forall i\in[2n^{a}]
, and
g_{i}=0
,
\forall i\notin[2n^{a}]
.
Note that
g
is non-increasing,
n^{a+o(1)}\cdot(\widetilde{p}_{j}g_{\widetilde{p}_{j}})=\widetilde{p}_{j}n^{a+o(1)}
, and
\|g\|_{2}=\sqrt{2n^{a}}
. Then we can use the same argument as Lemma
F.30
for
PartialMatrixUpdate
to prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\widetilde{p}_{j}n^{a+o(1)}=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\log n\cdot n^{a+o(1)}\cdot\|g\|_{2}\big)=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot n^{1.5a+o(1)}\big).

Part 2.
For the second equation, we let
g\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be

\displaystyle g_{i}=\begin{cases}n^{-\widetilde{a}},&\penalty\ i\leq n^{\widetilde{a}},\\
i^{-1},&\penalty\ n^{\widetilde{a}}<i\leq 2n^{a},\\
0,&\penalty\ i>2n^{a}.\end{cases}

Note that
g
is non-increasing, and
n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0}=n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}}\leq n^{2a}\cdot(p_{j}g_{p_{j}})
since analogous to Fact
F.11
we have that either
\widetilde{p}_{j}=0
or
\widetilde{p}_{j}\geq n^{\widetilde{a}}
. We also have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{n^{\widetilde{a}}}{n^{2\widetilde{a}}}+\int_{n^{\widetilde{a}}}^{2n^{a}}x^{-2}\mathrm{d}x=n^{-\widetilde{a}}+n^{-\widetilde{a}}-n^{-a}/2=O(n^{-\widetilde{a}}).

Then we can use the same argument as the Lemma
F.30
for
PartialMatrixUpdate
to prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{p}_{j}>0}=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\log n\cdot n^{2a}\|g\|_{2}\big)=O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\log n\cdot n^{2a-\widetilde{a}/2}\big).

Combine Part 1 and Part 2.
Using Lemma
E.33
and note that
n^{1.5a}\leq n^{1.5}
, we have that the expected amortized running time per iteration of
PartialVectorUpdate
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{T}(p_{j}n^{1+o(1)}+n^{2a}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{p_{j}>0})=

\displaystyle\penalty\ O\big((C_{4}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}+C_{5}/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}^{2})\cdot\log n\cdot(n^{1.5+o(1)}+n^{2a-\widetilde{a}/2})\big).

∎

F.7
Potential function
\psi

Figure 9:
\psi(x)
,
\psi(x)^{\prime}
and
\psi(x)^{\prime\prime}
. For
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}\in(0,1)
, and for simplicity we use
\epsilon
in the figures.

Lemma F.37

(Properties of function
\psi
, Lemma 5.10 of
[
CLS19
]
)
.

Let function
\psi
be defined as of Definition
F.4
. Then function
\psi
satisfies the following properties:

1. Symmetric:
\psi(-x)=\psi(x)
and
\psi(0)=0
;

2.
\psi(|x|)
is non-decreasing;

3.
|\psi^{\prime}(x)|=\Omega(1),\forall|x|\leq 1.5\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
;

4.
L_{1}:=\max_{x}\psi^{\prime}(x)=1
and
L_{2}:=\max_{x}\psi^{\prime\prime}(x)=1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
;

5.
\psi(x)
is a constant for
|x|\geq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}
.

Proof.

We can see that

\displaystyle\psi(x)^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\frac{|x|}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}&|x|\in[0,\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}]\\
\frac{2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}-|x|}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}&|x|\in(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}]\\
0&x\in(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},+\infty)\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi(x)^{\prime\prime}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}&x\in[0,\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}]\cup[-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},-\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}]\\
-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}&x\in(\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}]\cup[-\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},0]\\
0&x\in(2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}},+\infty)\end{cases}

From the
\psi(x)^{\prime}
and
\psi(x)^{\prime\prime}
, it is not hard to see that
\psi
satisfies the properties needed.
∎
